# Unfilter digest 11 — pregnancy, birth and the first weeks

Read on 2026-11-17. Share: voice/reddit-ur06.jsonl (396 quote records), voice/reddit-ur29.jsonl (432), and voice/reddit-ur30.jsonl (339): **1,167 quote records**, all read in full. Each ledger’s “What these posts add” section, the build README and the current findings’ structure and conclusions informed this digest.

These posts were selected because earlier rules excluded them. This is not a prevalence sample. Counts below count quote records or distinct quote ids, never people, pregnancies, independent stories, purchases or demand. Several records can be passages from one story, a reply or a follow-up. Crossposts can appear across these shares or the other digests.

All source quotations below reproduce the complete quote field, including spelling, punctuation, explicit language and source ellipses. Analysis and proposed changes are interpretation. Medical, medication, screening, legal, insurance and service-policy statements remain attributed accounts; no network verification was performed. No material was omitted or softened because of its subject.

The main change for the rewrite is scope: preparing for birth is only one part of these accounts. A parent may still be deciding whether to continue, may be recovering from loss, may be unable to function, or may be afraid that asking honestly for help will cost her safety or her baby. These situations need explanations in their own right, rather than being folded into a missed checklist.

Priority changes to the current findings:

- Add pregnancy-continuation decisions and loss care as substantive sections. Include certainty about abortion, ambivalence, regret, adoption and surrogacy without forcing one emotional outcome.
- Separate practical partner participation from reproductive coercion, violence, sexual pressure and threats. A task list does not answer all partner problems.
- Explain why reassurance may fail after loss, and why an app, scan, portal or test can create a new uncertainty. Some parents ask for all outcomes; others explicitly ask for positive stories only.
- Expand functioning, recovery and mental health: symptoms can affect continuation decisions; excellent birth and abundant help do not guarantee emotional well-being; sleep opportunity does not guarantee sleep.
- Make care navigation include trust, permission to call, wording, contradictory instructions, opening hours, transport, disability, language, housing and missing documents.
- Keep positive and negative experiences together. Helpful treatment, respectful care, involved partners and worthwhile paid help are concrete counterevidence to a uniformly adverse account.
- Keep money and requests separate from purchase intent for Mamivio. This share contains no demonstrated purchase or stated price for the proposed guide.

Evidence navigation: each full source quotation appears at its first relevant pattern below; later patterns and the Money/Requests registers link to it. “Further supporting ids” are additional relevant records, including some quoted fully elsewhere. Outliers repeat selected passages so the counterevidence can be read together.

Provenance: the UR06 positive induction account involving a named device and Gemini is retained as one author’s account, with no independent efficacy claim. UR29’s ledger identifies AI assistance with grammar/wording for [pbu29-0120] and [pbu29-0121]. The doula-training promoter in [pbu30-0050] is an interested professional, not a parent asking to buy a course or independent evidence that a course works. [pbu06-0039] and [pbu29-0329] have identical quote fields; both ids are retained for record coverage and represent duplicate wording, not two independent voices. The UR30 ledger’s burned ids are not records in this share and are not cited.

<a id="pattern-1"></a>
## 1. A wanted pregnancy can still be an open decision, and distress about pregnancy is not the same as regret about abortion.

**Current findings home:** §§1–2, 6, 9–10; add a dedicated reproductive-decision subsection.

**Assessment:** Extends; contradicts any assumption that the reader has already decided to continue.

**Current conclusion / wording to revise or extend:**

<!-- findings-quote -->
> The guide hypothesis now needs to address how women understand choices, ask for explanations, plan support around illness and recovery, and use information without a reassurance loop (§§2–9).
<!-- /findings-quote -->

**What should change:** Describe wanting motherhood, wanting this pregnancy, being physically able to continue, timing, and choosing abortion or adoption as distinct questions. Include uncertainty after planned conception and certainty about abortion; neither implies a single emotional outcome. Symptoms and requests for help still matter when pregnancy will end.

**Strong and distinctive evidence:**

## 2. Some partner problems are violence, sexual pressure or control of reproduction, rather than a failure to read or help.

**Current findings home:** §§4–6, 9–10, 17.1; add coercion and safety as its own seam.

**Assessment:** Extends the existing format limit and materially changes the partner interpretation.

**Current conclusion / wording to revise or extend:**

<!-- findings-quote -->
> Format helps the partner who wants to learn; it does not create the wish
<!-- /findings-quote -->

**What should change:** Keep violence, threats, nonconsensual ejaculation, pressure to abort or continue, forced exercise, sexual entitlement and control through housing or money distinct from ordinary disagreement. A task list cannot account for these accounts. Do not imply that the mother must educate or manage the person frightening her.

> I remember being in so much pain and my hand being so weak that I could barely move it. I tried making the movements he wanted, but I couldn't even finish the job. Eventually, he left and finished in the bathroom.
<!-- /source-quote -->

<a id="pbu30-0331"></a>
**[pbu30-0331]**

<!-- source-quote:pbu30-0331 -->
> Oh, it’s honestly shocking for me to read this. I thought what I was experiencing was just a normal reality that I had to accept, and that I was somehow failing as a woman because of all the pressure… I’m glad things are better for you, and I really appreciate you helping me see things more clearly.
<!-- /source-quote -->

<a id="pbu30-0346"></a>
**[pbu30-0346]**

<!-- source-quote:pbu30-0346 -->
> I didn’t want to live a pregnancy stressed or worried about whether or not my husband will harm himself. Will I be single mother, a widow? So I am angry. It’s hard to feel compassion towards him.
<!-- /source-quote -->

<a id="pbu30-0347"></a>
**[pbu30-0347]**

<!-- source-quote:pbu30-0347 -->
> Every time I mention I want to eat a food that is not healthy, he responds with “you can’t just eat unhealthy foods, think about baby.” But I feel no compassion for my nausea. I am trying hard to find foods to satisfy my hunger but that doesn’t make me feel gross. I feel alone and he basically told me we have to look at the bigger picture and forget about ourselves, which is true. But if I am not feeling supportive emotionally or if I am constantly having to deal with  my pregnancy symptoms on top of his depressive episodes, then what? Where do I stand? It is beyond stressful. He told me today I don’t make time for him, but I’ve literally been sick for 2 weeks with the flu.
<!-- /source-quote -->

<a id="pattern-3"></a>
## 3. Loss has its own physical recovery, care choices, grief and need for recognition.

**Current findings home:** §§2, 4–6, 9, 12–13; add loss and post-loss care.

**Assessment:** Extends and partly fills the acknowledged whole-unit loss gap.

**Current conclusion / wording to revise or extend:**

<!-- findings-quote -->
> Restoring surviving quotes broadens this account substantially, including birth procedures, reported coercion, recovery, feeding and mental health; it cannot recover words never extracted.
<!-- /findings-quote -->

**What should change:** The reread now supplies actual loss and termination accounts. Explain choices between waiting, medicines and procedures as the questions parents ask; preserve aftercare, exhaustion, lost plans, memorials and grief without assuming another pregnancy resolves it. Update the claim that no raw units have been reread.

**Strong and distinctive evidence:**

<a id="pbu06-0051"></a>
**[pbu06-0051]**

<!-- source-quote:pbu06-0051 -->
> I just feel completely lost, like I'm grieving something that hasn't happened yet. I feel like I'm mourning an imaginary loss. Our very real daughter is joining our family in just a few weeks, and we're juggling trying to prepare for her homecoming while also grieving what we thought life would be like and prepping for the very logistical things that may need to happen.
<!-- /source-quote -->

**[pbu06-0072]**

<!-- source-quote:pbu06-0072 -->

**[pbu06-0073]**

<!-- source-quote:pbu06-0073 -->

**[pbu06-0168]**

<!-- source-quote:pbu06-0168 -->

**[pbu06-0184]**

<!-- /source-quote -->

**[pbu06-0257]**

**[pbu06-0280]**

How normal is this? I've seen women on here with similar experiences, but I'm just in so much pain and feeling kind of lost.

<!-- /source-quote -->

**[pbu06-0359]**

<!-- /source-quote -->

The placenta didn't come out so I had to have a D&C as well.

<!-- /source-quote -->

<a id="pattern-4"></a>
## 4. After loss, feeling better, a good scan or a milestone can fail to reassure because the previous loss also seemed normal.

**Current findings home:** §§2, 4, 6, 10–11, 16.4, 17.6.

**Assessment:** Extends the existing reassurance-loop mechanism.

**Current conclusion / wording to revise or extend:**

<!-- findings-quote -->
> Checking, apps, scans, family stories, media and chatbots may soothe or extend a loop; some people seek direct information and others seek less.
<!-- /findings-quote -->

**What should change:** State why reassurance fails: no symptoms warned of an earlier loss; a heartbeat was previously followed by loss; ordinary symptom variation carries opposite meanings. Distinguish seeking companionship in uncertainty from asking someone to predict an outcome. A calendar can become a succession of dates to survive.

**Strong and distinctive evidence:**

<a id="pbu06-0016"></a>
**[pbu06-0016]**

<!-- source-quote:pbu06-0016 -->
> I suffered a missed miscarriage in September 25 and this is my second pregnancy.  
> 
> Please be realistic with me, is there no hope? In my head i have already told myself it's doomed.
<!-- /source-quote -->

**[pbu06-0019]**

<!-- source-quote:pbu06-0019 -->

> And now, five days postpartum, I’m sitting here staring into my daughter’s beautiful eyes, wondering how I got so lucky. I know that changing my lifestyle does not guarantee a successful pregnancy. Plenty of women experience miscarriages while living incredibly healthy lives, and pregnancy loss is not a personal failure. There are so many possible reasons for miscarriage, and sometimes we never get the answers we desperately want. But I do think that losing weight, giving up alcohol, and prioritizing our health gave my partner and me a better foundation going into this pregnancy. I cannot say that those changes are what made the difference, and I would never want anyone to read my story and feel responsible for a loss they experienced.

<a id="pbu29-0393"></a>
**[pbu29-0393]**

<!-- source-quote:pbu29-0393 -->
> She spent what felt like five minutes trying to find the heartbeat. I was starting to panic, but she reassured me that if she couldn’t find it on the Doppler, she would do an ultrasound. So I got dressed and we went to the ultrasound room, only to find out there was no sonographer there that day. The midwife decided to attempt a transabdominal ultrasound herself. I’ve only had transvaginal ultrasounds so far because of how small the baby is, and she told us she wasn’t very familiar with the ultrasound machine but would do her best.  
> She started moving the probe around and looking at the screen, and then she looked concerned. My husband and I couldn’t see what she was seeing, so we immediately started getting worried. She eventually turned the screen toward us, but the image was extremely blurry. She thought the machine might be on the wrong setting, so she called a doctor in to help. We waited another 5–10 minutes for him to come in. He knew the machine better than she did and was able to get a somewhat clearer image, but we still couldn’t see much. He tried to find the heartbeat and couldn’t. He tried again, and again, and again, and there was nothing.  
> At that point, I was crying and fully expecting the worst. Having had a miscarriage before, I immediately went back to that place mentally. Then, completely out of nowhere, he finally found cardiac activity. We could see the baby moving, and then we actually watched the baby do a full-on jump in my uterus. I have never felt so much fear and relief in such a short amount of time. Everything ended up being okay, but that appointment was genuinely one of the most stressful moments of my life. My baby was apparently just very determined to give us a heart attack today.
<!-- /source-quote -->

> I just found out today im pregnant with my rainbow baby. I took about 4 pee tests and they all came back with a faint line and one positive digital test. I took a test about 3ish days ago before I went out with my friends bc we were gonna be drinking and I just wanted to make sure and it was negative. I miscarried last June with my first pregnancy. It completely traumatized me and I was devastated. And today when I got these positive tests, I wanted to be excited so badly, and I feel so guilty, but right now I am scared to death. I’m so scared of getting the heartbreaking news there’s no heartbeat, im scared of going through the pain of having to tell everything that the gender reveal is off, im SO scared of getting bad news again. I honestly have no idea how many dpo I am bc I haven’t been tracking these past couple months, but my period is supposed to start tomorrow, so I’d guess im 3-4 weeks along. I have no symptoms yet, my boobs are a little sore, and I feel a little tired today but I feel like that’s bc I’ve been up since 5am lol. I just don’t know if this is a bad “gut feeling” or just genuine anxiety. I just want to cry, I want everything to be okay, I’ve been praying all day that this baby sticks with me, bc I truly can’t go through that pain again.💔 has anyone else experienced this? If so, how did you get through it. Bc I am so scared of losing another baby. 😭

<a id="pattern-5"></a>
## 5. Tests, portals and apps can create uncertainty through unexplained labels, contradictory dates or conflicting expert advice.

**Current findings home:** §§4–6, 10, 16.2, 17.4.

**Assessment:** Extends the existing account of rules without explanations.

**Current conclusion / wording to revise or extend:**

<!-- findings-quote -->
> the overload is often rules without reasons.
<!-- /findings-quote -->

**What should change:** Include what a result is measuring, what remains unknown, who will explain it, when follow-up occurs and whose advice conflicts. Preserve testing and termination pressure, ethnic interpretation, risk-number limitations and explicit requests for both good and bad outcomes. Reported probabilities and diagnostic claims are testimony.

**Strong and distinctive evidence:**

<a id="pattern-6"></a>
## 6. Knowing a contact number does not solve fear of calling, contradictory triage instructions or inaccessible follow-up.

**Current findings home:** §§4–6, 10, 16.2, 17.2–17.4.

**Assessment:** Extends; qualifies the contacts-page interpretation.

**Current conclusion / wording to revise or extend:**

<!-- findings-quote -->
> Interpretation: the gap is information as much as contacts.
<!-- /findings-quote -->

**What should change:** Add the practical act of asking: whom to call, what to say, whether the service is open, what to do after dismissal, how clinicians communicate, and how to seek an explanation. Some authors hesitate because earlier help-seeking made them feel wrong. Include positive listening and effective escalation beside failures.

**Strong and distinctive evidence:**

<a id="pbu06-0010"></a>
**[pbu06-0010]**

<!-- source-quote:pbu06-0010 -->
> A part of me wants to call now to make sure everything is okay before it gets much later. Another part of me doesn’t want to be the boy who cried wolf and I feel anxious and guilty about calling when I’m not having 5 minute apart contractions. I wish they had a nurses line or something 😭
<!-- /source-quote -->

And obviously things can be done because I’ve been absolutely pumped full of fluids and meds. Kind of upset that I was misled but happy I got in before anything escalated further. Thank you guys

<a id="pattern-7"></a>
## 7. Medication choices weigh maternal function, withdrawal, infant fears, feeding and access at the same time.

**Current findings home:** §§2, 4–6, 9–10.

**Assessment:** Extends the practical meaning of personalization and functioning.

**Current conclusion / wording to revise or extend:**

<!-- findings-quote -->
> This reader is also trying to be believed, retain a say in birth decisions, afford care and remain able to function.
<!-- /findings-quote -->

**What should change:** Include stopping, tapering, restarting, side effects, waiting for advice and fear of having already harmed the baby. Do not turn authors' risk beliefs, reported neonatal withdrawal or family autism warnings into established effects. Treatment can help and can feel unsuccessful; prescribing reassurance and internet searching can pull in opposite directions.

**Strong and distinctive evidence:**

<a id="pbu06-0031"></a>
**[pbu06-0031]**

<!-- source-quote:pbu06-0031 -->
> When my first was born last year I was not prepared for the way postpartum would affect me. The first few weeks were debilitating. I had constant anxiety, depression and developed intrusive thoughts that terrified me. I became afraid of my own mind and started questioning everything. Eventually I was diagnosed with PPD, PPA and OCD. I started Zoloft, went to therapy, and over time things slowly got better. It definitely wasn’t overnight, but I eventually felt like myself again.
<!-- /source-quote -->

<a id="pattern-8"></a>
## 8. Parents describe dependence, exposure guilt and difficulty quitting, including help that amounts only to an instruction to stop.

**Current findings home:** §§2, 4–6, 9, 12; a named substance-use subsection is needed.

**Assessment:** Extends the historical substance-use gap.

**Current conclusion / wording to revise or extend:**

<!-- findings-quote -->
> The original exclusion of emergency, loss and crisis units left the birth itself and difficult first weeks under-recorded.
<!-- /findings-quote -->

**What should change:** Record nicotine, cannabis, alcohol, opioid/MAT access and medication withdrawal in the authors' own terms. Include successful quitting and ongoing difficulty. The mechanism may be shame, grief, panic, lack of access or lost insurance; commitment to the baby does not by itself explain whether quitting feels possible.

**Strong and distinctive evidence:**

All with no avail. It was not getting better and i was in complete misery and pain. i did something im not proud of. i broke down and i took 1 200 mg tablet of ibuprofen 😭 and i knew i wasn’t supposed to. i was on google for 2 hours looking at the risks and any alternatives that i could take that were safe. but i took it anyway. and it almost instantly gave me relief. and i felt so much better physically. but since last night i can’t stop thinking about how selfish it was and the harm i may have possibly done to my baby 😭 please has anyone ever done this and what was your experience. did your baby end up being okay 😭 i have an appointment with my OB on monday where im going to discuss it with her but in the mean time i just need to hear from other moms

<a id="pattern-9"></a>
## 9. Physical suffering can remove the ability to work, parent or continue a deeply wanted pregnancy.

**Current findings home:** §§1–2, 6, 9–10, 17.2.

**Assessment:** Extends the broken-trimester-script conclusion.

**Current conclusion / wording to revise or extend:**

<!-- findings-quote -->
> The broken week-by-week script includes persistent pain, exhaustion, vomiting and incontinence, repeated remedies and disrupted sleep.
<!-- /findings-quote -->

**What should change:** Describe function and cumulative suffering, including HG-driven termination decisions after IVF and desperation in healthcare workers. Relief at delivery is not guaranteed. Ordinary digestion, gum pain, infections, itching, rhinitis, dizziness and disabling pain all belong; repeated remedies and successful treatment should remain visible.

**Strong and distinctive evidence:**

<a id="pattern-10"></a>
## 10. Planned conception, years of wanting a baby and a loving family do not guarantee joy, connection or a tolerable mental state.

**Current findings home:** §§1–3, 6, 9–10.

**Assessment:** Extends the stock excitement script and the existing fear-and-identity account.

**Current conclusion / wording to revise or extend:**

<!-- findings-quote -->
> You must be so excited.
<!-- /findings-quote -->

**What should change:** Separate dread, depression, dissociation, intrusive thoughts, eating-disorder recurrence, body grief, past childhood trauma and loss of identity. Some parents feel guilty for distress itself because they believe it harms the baby. Support and gratitude can coexist with suicidal thoughts; preserve their exact wording and do not infer diagnoses.

**Strong and distinctive evidence:**

<a id="pbu06-0004"></a>
**[pbu06-0004]**

<!-- source-quote:pbu06-0004 -->
> I don't know what to do, I feel like a prisoner in my own house. I hate the hair and how she's always in the way. I want to be able to walk around the house freely without dogs all up in the baby and i's business. I've been crying about it nonstop and it sucks, I know people will think I'm a monster because you're not allowed to have negative feelings towards dogs, I need help.
<!-- /source-quote -->

<a id="pbu06-0013"></a>
**[pbu06-0013]**

<!-- source-quote:pbu06-0013 -->
> I feel like it’s normal and mature to acknowledge all of it, like anything else but it’s like I’m not allowed.

<a id="pbu06-0047"></a>
**[pbu06-0047]**

<!-- source-quote:pbu06-0047 -->
> I have felt suicidal at times, but I am trying to remain alive. I’m nervous to give birth, nervous for my body to go through the trauma, and nervous to make this life changing decision. My parents promised financial support during my time off work because my employer does not give much for maternity leave. Now I am stressed about money, without a family, no support from baby dad, and days away from giving birth. Will I be okay? What do I do with this baby?
<!-- /source-quote -->

<a id="pbu06-0056"></a>
**[pbu06-0056]**

<!-- source-quote:pbu06-0056 -->

<a id="pbu06-0057"></a>
**[pbu06-0057]**

<a id="pbu06-0138"></a>
**[pbu06-0138]**

<!-- source-quote:pbu06-0138 -->

<!-- /source-quote -->

<a id="pbu06-0170"></a>
**[pbu06-0170]**

<!-- source-quote:pbu06-0170 -->
> He doesn’t seem to understand that for a short while during this pregnancy, I just need him to rise to the occasion, support me and not wait for me.  I need him to take care of me. He even said our first appointment today I was “tense” and didn’t seem excited when in reality I was riddled with anxiety. I’m going to bed feeling alone. No matter what I say or do, we’re disconnected and it hurts. I’ve never felt this way in a pregnancy before, especially since I was previously married. It’s isolating and confronting. **[pbu06-0196]**

**[pbu29-0120]**

I want to be clear that I don’t intend to act on them they’re more like an escape in my head when everything feels too much.
<!-- /source-quote -->

<a id="pbu29-0121"></a>
**[pbu29-0121]**

<!-- source-quote:pbu29-0121 -->
> I’ve already had 3 months off this year, so I feel incredibly guilty even considering being off again. My employer shouldn’t have to keep suffering because I’m struggling.

>

<!-- /source-quote -->

<a id="pbu29-0132"></a>
**[pbu29-0132]**

<!-- source-quote:pbu29-0132 -->
> 1) If I give birth myself, I fear that I will not be able to handle the changes that take place in my body. Not in a superficial context. I recognize that many unfamiliar changes happen to the female body during pregnancy and after childbirth. I fear that being in pain will be PTSD for me. I fear that I will resent my husband and child, not because I blame them, but because I’ll once again feel uncomfortable in my skin, I’ll feel pain in areas that I felt pain for most of my childhood, adolescent, and teenage life, and I’ll snap. I’ve come so far in my healing and I am so scared to undo my progress.
<!-- /source-quote -->

<a id="pbu29-0133"></a>
**[pbu29-0133]**

<!-- source-quote:pbu29-0133 -->
> 2) If I get pregnant, I am terrified of risking my and/or my baby’s health. <!-- /source-quote -->

<a id="pbu29-0134"></a>
**[pbu29-0134]**

<!-- source-quote:pbu29-0134 -->

<!-- /source-quote -->

<a id="pbu29-0369"></a>
**[pbu29-0369]**

> I am scheduled for a c-section on Monday. I’ve had a pretty decent pregnancy aside from severe depression and ambivalence and morning sickness in the first trimester. This baby was very much wanted and planned for. Something shifted in me overnight it seems like about a week after I found out I was pregnant where I just became totally ambivalent about the pregnancy and struggled severely with depression and not wanting to be on this planet anymore. Things got better a few months ago and I’m doing ok now thanks to weekly therapy sessions. However, the past few days and certainly today I have been non-stop crying about how much my life is going to change (not being able to nap when I want, veging out on the couch after a stressful work day, going to get a blowout or a massage on a whim, etc.) and my husband and I’s dynamic changing. My husband is insanely supportive and I know I will still be able to do the things I listed above, but it will now require extra planning or waiting until the evening when he’s home from work. This is our first so it’s been my husband and I only for the last 14 years just traveling and being DINKS. I love my husband dearly and I enjoy our time together so much so I am terrified how much that is going to change. I’m looking for any advice or encouragement to get through this weekend.
<!-- /source-quote -->

<a id="pbu29-0403"></a>
**[pbu29-0403]**

<!-- source-quote:pbu29-0403 -->
> I am 14 weeks, going into 15. First baby, and not sure what to expect. I've always been thin, never had huge weight fluctuations, and so this feels very new and odd to me to suddenly be feeling tightness in my clothes. I had what I assume was some light bloating during the first trimester, but I've just reached the point where some of my clothes are starting not to fit, my pants are too tight around my belly, and sweats that used to be loose are fitting snug. It's hard for me to really picture what my belly is about to do, or how big I'm about to get, I feel like I can't wrap my head around it, and I feel sort of in denial even now when my belly is just starting to swell up noticeably (to me atleast). I guess I'm just hoping for others experiences as first time moms, how quickly did your belly start getting big from this point (14/15 weeks) onward, how was it adjusting to that? I also have been trying to keep it as private as I can, I haven't told many people yet and I'm wondering how long I'll be able to keep it hidden before my belly gets too noticable. Honestly, just anyone who can relate or can share a bit would be much appreciated, I feel like the first noticeable "bump" made it feel so real so fast. It's so early and Im already feeling a little bit of that over puffed balloon feeling in my belly, which is making me a little anxious about the weeks ahead. Also any tips for alleviating that? Ive found rubbing my belly kinda helps temporarily, but I know it's only going to get more intense from here

<a id="pattern-11"></a>
## 11. An easy birth and abundant support can be followed by severe distress, insomnia or fear of being alone with the baby.

**Current findings home:** §§2, 5–6, 9–10; recovery, sleep and mental-health subsections.

**Assessment:** Extends; contradicts any causal promise that preparation or help prevents distress.

**Current conclusion / wording to revise or extend:**

<!-- findings-quote -->
> A healthy baby or an outwardly successful birth does not settle what the mother experienced; relief and improvement also appear.
<!-- /findings-quote -->

**What should change:** Distinguish sleep opportunity from ability to sleep, ordinary exhaustion from authors' reported crises, immediate bonding from love expressed through care, and normalizing information from permission to seek help. Include fathers' rough handling and shame, barriers to disclosure, later improvement and positive second/third postpartum experiences. The two-week baby-blues frame can itself delay asking.

**Strong and distinctive evidence:**

> My labor and birth experience was about as perfect as I could have hoped. I had to be induced at 38 weeks due to a sudden onset of preeclampsia, but the whole process was great. I got my epidural early, I never even felt my contractions, and I slept through all 18 hours of my labor. I pushed nine times across 27 minutes and it was completely painless, I only got one small first degree tear. I feel like I couldn't have hoped for anything better. Even my recovery has been perfect. I had minor cramps that were treated with midol for two days and only bled for a week and a half.
<!-- /source-quote -->

<a id="pbu29-0136"></a>
**[pbu29-0136]**

<!-- source-quote:pbu29-0136 -->
> But even with all of that, I still cried all day every day for the first week of his life, and last night I broke down crying with him in my arms and told my husband I didn't think I could live any longer. Now I have an appointment with my OBGYN first thing Monday to get some help, but it all feels so stupid and dramatic. I got everything I wanted and I'm still depressed, how is that possible? I feel so ungrateful and like I'm traumatizing my own baby because he's had to witness all of the break downs.
<!-- /source-quote -->

<a id="pbu29-0140"></a>
**[pbu29-0140]**

<!-- source-quote:pbu29-0140 -->
> The baby blues quickly turned into PPD. I was recovering from a 3L bleed, a broken finger, a vaginal birth, and my baby would exclusively chest sleep. I started getting severe nightmares whenever I could sleep, and at 4 weeks PP my husband told me I needed to see the GP. As I was experiencing >! passive suicidal ideation !< I was referred to every perinatal support service in my area and we implemented a few changes to our routine at home to help.
<!-- /source-quote -->

<a id="pbu29-0141"></a>
**[pbu29-0141]**

<!-- source-quote:pbu29-0141 -->
> EMDR was the biggest help for me during the fourth trimester. It was like night and day before and after my first session, and slowly I was able to start talking about the birth without crying. Over time, I moved from weekly appointments to monthly as a check-in and have kept this up for 2 years now. I see a mental health OT that specialises in Matrescence and Motherhood. A big part of this journey has also been looking at how I see motherhood as a result of my childhood, and doing work there.
<!-- /source-quote -->

<a id="pbu29-0142"></a>
**[pbu29-0142]**

<!-- source-quote:pbu29-0142 -->
> When I found out I was pregnant, my OT helped me articulate my beliefs and feelings so I could communicate them to my midwife. Sensory regulation therapy helped me to work out what leads me to feeling overwhelmed/overstimulated, and my biggest triggers (or "smallest cups") are sound and touch. I had started using earplugs for sleep just after the fourth trimester, but have continued every night since and it has improved my quality of sleep so much. My OT helped me put together a "connection kit" of tools/resources I can use when I start feeling overwhelmed or touched out. I took this kit with me to the hospital.
<!-- /source-quote -->

<a id="pbu29-0145"></a>
**[pbu29-0145]**

<!-- source-quote:pbu29-0145 -->
> Hi everyone! As above states, I’m a FTM and 5 days PP. The baby blues hit in full swing two days ago and I’m really scared. I’m scared of how I feel, I’m scared it’ll feel like this forever, and I’m scared of the future. I love my daughter and she has been amazing. At the same time, I feel scared that I’ll regret the decision to have a baby. I have horrible anxiety. I’m still caring for her, meeting my needs, etc. I don’t feel detached from her, but rather just like these overwhelming mood shifts. I feel so, so worried this will lead to PPD. Or I will actually regret having a baby. Like I’ll regret the life I left behind.
<!-- /source-quote -->

<a id="pbu29-0146"></a>
**[pbu29-0146]**

<!-- source-quote:pbu29-0146 -->
> I also have a lot of anxiety about being away from him. I want him close 24/7. I also have these intrusive thoughts that he will walk away one day if I don’t ever feel better from postpartum.
<!-- /source-quote -->

<a id="pbu29-0147"></a>
**[pbu29-0147]**

<!-- source-quote:pbu29-0147 -->
> I’m 8 wks post partum and am having increasing difficulty sleeping. I have moved to a room by myself. My husband and baby are in another room. He’s been taking the night shift to feed the baby essentially leaving me to sleep from about 11:30PM to 7 AM. I do the last dream feed of the night around 10:30PM. Also, I’m completely unable to daytime nap.   
> I pump during daytime only (never did it at night) and I stopped breastfeeding around week 2. I like sleep too much to spend hours awake at night pumping, breastfeeding and then bottle feeding. So nothing should be holding me back from sleeping, right ?
<!-- /source-quote -->

<a id="pbu29-0154"></a>
**[pbu29-0154]**

<!-- source-quote:pbu29-0154 -->
> Dad starts work at 6.30am, so he gets up at 5.15, finishes at 4.30pm, gets back home at 5pm - when he was on paternity, he did 75% of the night shift, and even said he didn't mind as he fell back to sleep easily. I did most nappy changes, and the occasional feed. I was sleeping really well, felt really well rested. He said, "When I go back to work, this will be all on you. You get more chance to sleep in until Toddler wakes up, and they have naps." - I didn't like the sound of that, but didn't argue as I don't work...
<!-- /source-quote -->

<a id="pbu29-0155"></a>
**[pbu29-0155]**

<!-- source-quote:pbu29-0155 -->
> At this point, he woke up and started telling me off for being "snappy" - I said I'd tried to wake him and I needed extra help even if it was just the feed around 12.30am because I was struggling with sleep. I started reading comments from a thread on here where the "shift" system seemed popular; he took offence at being "compared", said he's cycling to work and needs not to be exhausted. Then got passive aggressive and said he'd do "all the night feeds and just be exhausted." - in the morning, he said "You should just ASK for help!" (he's autistic and struggles "reading between the lines".) and he's decided to... go back to his dad's! Because I'm a "miserable witch"! So now I've asked for help and am back here struggling anyways.
<!-- /source-quote -->

<a id="pbu29-0156"></a>
**[pbu29-0156]**

<!-- source-quote:pbu29-0156 -->
> Further details: I've been in Talking Therapies for quite some time - since April this year, as April is a difficult time of year for me as it's my late partner's death date (20th) and birthday (30th). I'm awaiting ADHD diagnosis through a provider my GP accepts for care. I suspect I've been depressed for a long time, but really mask it. When I think about how I "normally" feel, it's mostly just nothing. I have had thoughts of ideation, and in particularly bad moments when feeling let down by him, have left the room with intentions but as soon as I hold anything, the thoughts disappear and I sort of come back to myself - he says this is a "threat" and he doesn't "like" that I do it. But the fact is there is a split moment where I feel I'd just be better off not dealing with any of this - the pain I feel from him rejecting me and leaving yet again is so much that I just don't want it.
<!-- /source-quote -->

<a id="pbu29-0390"></a>
**[pbu29-0390]**

<!-- source-quote:pbu29-0390 -->
> For background, I have Bipolar and this is my first pregnancy. I manage my symptoms very well, you wouldn’t even know I had it unless you’ve known me for years. I worry about the hormonal shift during the first few months and my disorder needs to prioritize sleep. I’ve read that the nighttime doula can provide nighttime feedings so you can sleep. My partner is the sole provider financially so this support during the week would be amazing. He doesn’t have any family and I don’t have either of my parents. My sisters live an hour away each and have their own little ones and full time jobs. My oldest sister did say she was going to provide a lot of support because she knows we don’t have our mom here anymore. Plus she’s been in the mental health field for over 20 years.
<!-- /source-quote -->

<a id="pbu30-0062"></a>
**[pbu30-0062]**

<!-- source-quote:pbu30-0062 -->
> I've had a rough time with PPD and PTSD from a very scary and traumatic birth experience. For the first 6 months he was very supportive but it has turned to resentment, I'm signed off work and in his eyes I sit around and do nothing all day. I care for the kids and the house but not to the standard he now expects. I'm in very intense therapy (EMDR) that has a big physical and mental impact on me.
<!-- /source-quote -->

<a id="pbu30-0070"></a>
**[pbu30-0070]**

<!-- source-quote:pbu30-0070 -->
> I found myself getting rough with her because of the crying during early morning feeds and don't feel like a very proud first time dad tbh. I have been more rough than I'd like to admit.
<!-- /source-quote -->

<a id="pbu30-0071"></a>
**[pbu30-0071]**

<!-- source-quote:pbu30-0071 -->
> I am so sleep deprived and so is my wife. I find myself loving my wife more than ever and feel as though I have never wanted my wife more than ever until now. If I had to guess i probably developed some type of separation anxiety with my wife because my wife has given me plenty of opportunities to get some sleep, step away, recoup and come back. But it leaves me feeling too bad for her because I really don't want her feeling like she can't come to me for a hand.
<!-- /source-quote -->

<a id="pbu30-0073"></a>
**[pbu30-0073]**

<!-- source-quote:pbu30-0073 -->
> I spoke to her about everything that had been bothering and worrying me. I wasn't accusatory, but I did tell her that even her own mother is worried about leaving our son alone with her. And i am worried too. I expected her to get defensive or deny it, but instead she just told me that she doesn't think she's cut out to be a mother. She said she doesn't see herself as a mother and doesn't feel like she fits that role. She says that she feels uncomfortable with being alone with the baby.
<!-- /source-quote -->

<a id="pbu30-0076"></a>
**[pbu30-0076]**

<!-- source-quote:pbu30-0076 -->
> I had days that I sawthe things that weren't there, I hallucinated in the first few months like 4 times because of colicky baby and I was sleeping like 3 hours in total 4 fucking days. Not joking. I thought I'm gonna lose my mind. I even thought to kill myself and tried to plan the next steps for my baby. But it was so tiring, I couldn't do that so i couldn't kill myself because of that. I was so tiring even to plan what's gonna happen to my baby after I die, so that's why I didn't die...
<!-- /source-quote -->

<a id="pbu30-0077"></a>
**[pbu30-0077]**

<!-- source-quote:pbu30-0077 -->
> Oh btw I moved to a new country when I was 9 months pregnant. To a country I can't speak the language. I was all alone when I was pregnant and mostly I was all alone in a hospital also when I was pregnant in another country they I moved there 3 years ago again all alone and I was alone in the hospital bed rest for 10 weeks.
<!-- /source-quote -->

<a id="pbu30-0104"></a>
**[pbu30-0104]**

<!-- source-quote:pbu30-0104 -->
> I just had my second baby and I’m suddenly realizing my “first time mom” stress was actually just raging, untreated postpartum anxiety. And now I feel really sad for that last version of myself.
> 
> So in case anyone is a first time mom and needs to hear this: It’s apparently NOT normal to constantly fear that you or your baby are going to die, to cry hysterically on a daily basis, to feel like your body is on fire every time your baby cries, or to feel like a negligent abusive mother just for putting your baby down. You’re not a sucky mom and you’re not “just hormonal”! Just FYI!!! Who knew!!! (Not me!)
<!-- /source-quote -->

<a id="pbu30-0105"></a>
**[pbu30-0105]**

<!-- source-quote:pbu30-0105 -->
> First time mom of a 1 week old. My labor/delivery was a bit traumatic, lots of things going wrong and then baby girl had to be resuscitated after an APGAR score of 2. I know birth can’t be planned but the things I was hoping for couldn’t happen, especially the uninterrupted golden hour & delayed cord clamping because of how quickly she needed to be whisked away.
<!-- /source-quote -->

<a id="pbu30-0106"></a>
**[pbu30-0106]**

<!-- source-quote:pbu30-0106 -->
> I’ve definitely had the sundown scaries because when nighttime hits my anxiety skyrockets, and I just feel so sad. I just want to cry without knowing exactly why. I just feel so bad. Daytime is better but it’s still so hard. I love this little girl so much but at the same time I really miss how things were when it was just us, and I feel so bad for feeling that way. I told my doula about the sundown scaries I’ve been feeling and she suggested bringing it up to my doctor and mentally prepare that postpartum depression is a possibility, she made it clear that she wasn’t saying I have it but just to note that medication and treatment could possibly be a thing in the future so if it does get to that I’m not too surprised or in denial of it (she said it better than this but that’s the gist of it that I got).
<!-- /source-quote -->

<a id="pbu30-0108"></a>
**[pbu30-0108]**

<!-- source-quote:pbu30-0108 -->
> Her birth was beautiful, easy, and quick. Everything went great stay at the hospital was fabulous!
>

I remember one time finding my baby on the couch stuck crying from losing consciousness. It was 2 weeks of hell, I was suicidal, wasn’t eating or sleeping. I felt in a daze-crying every morning. 
> 
> I ended up in the psychward for over 2 weeks, it helped me I got better I’m on medication for life now, and sleeping pills for my insomnia. I’m finally back to 80% of myself.
<!-- /source-quote -->

<a id="pbu30-0109"></a>
**[pbu30-0109]**

<a id="pattern-12"></a>
## 12. Birth choices depend on pain relief, medication timing, staffing, admission and prior injury as well as preferences.

**Current findings home:** §§2, 4–6, 9–10, 17.2.

**Assessment:** Extends; qualifies flexibility and a dated-plan interpretation.

**Current conclusion / wording to revise or extend:**

<!-- findings-quote -->
> Flexibility alone does not explain a good or difficult birth.
<!-- /findings-quote -->

**What should change:** Show what a planned option depends on and what can happen when that dependency fails. Include requests for induction, refusal of induction, desire for VBAC, requests for a planned section, fear of both routes, unpredictable labor and recovery comparisons. Neither intervention nor avoiding it is consistently the desired or positive outcome.

**Strong and distinctive evidence:**

<a id="pbu06-0023"></a>
**[pbu06-0023]**

<!-- source-quote:pbu06-0023 -->
> I’m worried an epidural would cause me a lot of stress and the fact that I have naturally low blood pressure, that can get extra low during pregnancy, worries me as well. I can sit at 80/50s now while pregnant.
<!-- /source-quote -->

<a id="pbu06-0024"></a>
**[pbu06-0024]**

<!-- source-quote:pbu06-0024 -->
> I do have a deep fear that my panic and anxiety will cause massive complications also if I chose an unmedicated birth. Will I pass out while in labor? Will I throw up so hard that I dissect an artery?? Things of that nature. I would like some perspective from people who have a similar type of mindset and experience, and what they did or chose with their labor.
<!-- /source-quote -->

<a id="pbu06-0035"></a>
**[pbu06-0035]**

<!-- source-quote:pbu06-0035 -->
> Our baby's Apgar score was 7/10. They were bluish and silent. At around two minutes old, they began making little whimpering sounds. At first we thought perhaps the placenta was still supplying enough oxygen, so there simply wasn't much urgency to fully "arrive" in the world yet.
> 
> The placenta was born within ten minutes. Our baby gradually pinked up, but still wasn't breathing consistently. The pulse oximeter showed oxygen levels below the desired range, and later they remained too low even with supplemental oxygen.
<!-- /source-quote -->

<a id="pbu06-0036"></a>
**[pbu06-0036]**

<!-- source-quote:pbu06-0036 -->
> At that point, the midwives decided to call an ambulance. We didn't panic because they stayed remarkably calm throughout. They reassured us there was no immediate danger to our baby's life, but that they should be assessed in the hospital.
<!-- /source-quote -->

<a id="pbu06-0037"></a>
**[pbu06-0037]**

<!-- source-quote:pbu06-0037 -->
> Meanwhile, the midwives took care of me. I hadn't torn, but I needed a few stitches to optimize healing. I couldn't urinate afterward, and because I wanted to get to the hospital as quickly as possible, I asked for a catheter instead of waiting. It wasn't the thick tube I'd imagined—just a very small, thin catheter designed for women. It certainly wasn't pleasant, but it was nowhere near as uncomfortable as I'd expected.
<!-- /source-quote -->

<a id="pbu06-0038"></a>
**[pbu06-0038]**

<!-- source-quote:pbu06-0038 -->
> Looking back, we would absolutely make the same choice again. I'm so grateful that we chose this path. Most of all, I'm deeply thankful to our midwives, who guided us through every step with such calmness, kindness, and confidence. They even came to visit us again on Sunday, just to see how we were doing. ❤️
<!-- /source-quote -->

<a id="pbu06-0093"></a>
**[pbu06-0093]**

> 
> My concerns about having an epidural are largely about it being coordinated properly, I’ve heard stories of women not getting to have one due to timings and what exactly does ‘early’ imply?
<!-- /source-quote -->

<a id="pbu06-0095"></a>
**[pbu06-0095]**

**[pbu06-0097]**

By the time it was actively used in the delivery room, the environment became very hectic, and the physical sensation was one of incredibly intense pressure. <a id="pbu06-0106"></a>

**[pbu06-0109]**

It’s absolute agony, no waves that I can breathe through, just constant pain that somehow gets worse consistently. I ask for an epidural and get told it’ll be 2-3 hours. I am beside myself with pain, feel totally panicked and out of control. Midwife suggests paracetamol(?!) and tells me that the contractions still aren’t strong enough to get baby here.
<!-- /source-quote -->

<!-- /source-quote -->

<a id="pbu06-0134"></a>
**[pbu06-0134]**

> They then did a test dose of the anaesthetic and this is where everything went south. The first 10 minutes was the relief I have been needing and I was calm for a while. After the 20 mins mark from when the test dose was given, the loss of sensation has gone up to my neck, i couldn’t swallow my spit anymore and my tongue felt swollen. Not long after, I noticed that everyone has started scrambling but trying to hide the panic from me. Then, there are a lot more doctors and nurses in the room including the head of anaesthetic and the head nurse of L&D. At this point, I started to doze off and the head nurse was trying her best to keep me awake as I was starting to lose consciousness.
<!-- /source-quote -->

**[pbu06-0135]**

<!-- /source-quote -->

<a id="pbu29-0126"></a>

> I had a c-section. It was literally the worst experience of my life. I was sliced open and  felt every puncture in every layer, felt my ribs, being pushed so my baby could be removed from my uterus, and every stitch required to jigsaw me back together. For months, the experience of my c-section overshadowed the birth of my son and I didn’t feel like it was worthwhile, a now I just don’t ask myself if it was worth it anymore. If there was some horrible magic that took me back to that point in order to have my child, I honestly don’t know how I’d respond.
<!-- /source-quote -->

<a id="pbu29-0127"></a>
**[pbu29-0127]**

<!-- source-quote:pbu29-0127 -->
> Now, it’s been 2 years and I’m pregnant again. I’m praying that I won’t have to have another c-section because I’m terrified of a repeat. I’m willing to give my body another try, but if I have another surgical birth, I’m never doing this again. There was NOTHING natural about my experience. I hesitate to even call it birth, and it irks my nerves to see the bleeding hearts come to my aid with unsolicited platitudes: “every birth is natural,” etc.  but it seems like I’m alone in this.
<!-- /source-quote -->

<a id="pbu29-0128"></a>
**[pbu29-0128]**

<!-- source-quote:pbu29-0128 -->
> I was a birth doula and worked in a hospital for years before I even got pregnant. Seeing so many pleasant vaginal births definitely informed my expectations and added on to my disappointment going into my c-section, but the actual experience felt like violence. Perhaps what I experienced wasn’t the norm, but I don’t see how anyone could consider c-section natural and it seems like I’m truly alone in this.
<!-- /source-quote -->

<a id="pbu29-0143"></a>
**[pbu29-0143]**

<!-- source-quote:pbu29-0143 -->
> My baby was healthy and I had a grade 1 tear with no bleeding, which we were all very excited about up until I started hemorrhaging from my uterus. My midwife had been prepared for this and there was a clear plan in place. She told my husband to press the call button, the room filled with people and the PPH trolley, and I was given a rundown of what would happen: if they got it under control, great, at 1L of blood loss I would go to theatre, and if it continued I would be looking at a hysterectomy. They got the bleed under control just past 900ML.
<!-- /source-quote -->

<a id="pbu29-0144"></a>

<!-- source-quote:pbu29-0144 -->

It was the birth I needed and feels very cathartic after all of the work, research and reading I did put in. The team of doctors and nurses who looked after me were incredible, and I have 6 weeks of home visits now with my midwife for support.
<!-- /source-quote -->

<!-- /source-quote -->

<a id="pbu29-0235"></a>
**[pbu29-0235]**

<!-- source-quote:pbu29-0235 -->

They said I could go home and come back in the morning for my c-section. So I reluctantly agreed but I was scared and so foggy that I didn’t know what to do, I just wanted it to be over. I wanted to stop feeling the way I was. Keep in mind; the induction had not started, they had not broken my water or anything of that nature.

My baby was in the NICU because I couldn’t do the one thing that was supposed to come so naturally to me. I failed my baby and because of me he had to be cared for by other people other than me.

I have a c-section scar and shelf for nothing. I went through major surgery for nothing when I could’ve just pushed my baby out like I wanted to. All of it was unnecessary. > A birth doula - I wasn’t sure what a doula even entailed but I went with my gut and hired one early in my pregnancy and I’m so glad I did! She was such a good source of support in late third trimester and during/after birth. It can be so stressful when you’re trying to figure out if you should induce or wait (especially when you’re 35+ and going past 40w) and she helped talk me through it. Having her there for the start of my labor and through birth was incredibly comforting. She knew exactly what to do at each stage, how to advocate for me, and how to coach me through the difficult moments. Pretty sure her latching my baby right after birth was a big reason breastfeeding went so smoothly too! Highly recommend a doula if you can - I found it affordable and well worth it.

It’s definitely a privilege to have this setup and has made me realize how much of a difference an involved husband makes in a woman’s postpartum experience.

> Back story- This is my (34F) first pregnancy and I had an induction at 39 weeks due to being on twice a day lovenox injections from a history of blood clots. Anesthesia requires you to be off of the injections for a minimum of 24 hours prior to an epidural due to the risk of spinal hematoma, thus why I had an induction planned so I could stop by shots 24 hours prior. My entire “birth plan” was just: epidural. I know my pain tolerance isn’t great and just wanted the option to have one if things got too intense.
<!-- /source-quote -->

<a id="pbu30-0232"></a>
**[pbu30-0232]**

<!-- source-quote:pbu30-0232 -->
> I begged the nurse to call anesthesia and ask if they would give me an epidural an hour early. They declined. At that moment my biggest fear with this pregnancy came true, I was going to have to have this baby naturally. I reverted inwards, emotionless, shocked and felt so alone despite my partner and the incredible staff being there. I felt like the entire night I was on the verge of ‘suffering’ which I recognize sounds dramatic but with ensuing pain control to nothing working that’s the only thing I can compare it to. With no other options (I didn’t want nitric bc I just wanted to focus on getting him out..) I started to push. At 6:45 anesthesia came in to prep for my epidural, just as he was crowning and it was too late.

I’ve never felt more dismissed for something that I have considered to be significant and traumatic.

**Also reproduced in earlier patterns:** [pbu29-0067](#pbu29-0067) · [pbu29-0135](#pbu29-0135) · [pbu29-0227](#pbu29-0227) · [pbu29-0228](#pbu29-0228) · [pbu29-0252](#pbu29-0252) · [pbu29-0402](#pbu29-0402) · [pbu29-0404](#pbu29-0404) · [pbu29-0413](#pbu29-0413) · [pbu30-0105](#pbu30-0105) · [pbu30-0164](#pbu30-0164) · [pbu30-0174](#pbu30-0174) · [pbu30-0182](#pbu30-0182) · [pbu30-0241](#pbu30-0241) · [pbu30-0254](#pbu30-0254) · [pbu30-0285](#pbu30-0285) · [pbu30-0297](#pbu30-0297) · [pbu30-0337](#pbu30-0337).

<a id="pattern-13"></a>
## 13. Consent requires understandable language, timely questions, respected refusals and preparation for trauma triggers.

**Current findings home:** §§4–6, 9–11; consent and being-believed subsections.

**Assessment:** Extends the decision-and-care-experience conclusion.

**Current conclusion / wording to revise or extend:**

<!-- findings-quote -->
> The decision and care experience need their own treatment.
<!-- /findings-quote -->

**What should change:** Specify timing while medicated or immobilized, translation, access to wanted pain relief, minimizing pain before procedures, and examination triggers after sexual assault. A parent can thank the staff and still describe a traumatic event. A good baby outcome does not retroactively provide consent or repair the parent's account.

**Strong and distinctive evidence:**

<a id="pbu06-0092"></a>
**[pbu06-0092]**

<!-- source-quote:pbu06-0092 -->
> In my first labour, some difficulties were experienced, a midwife read my notes and recognised the labour needed to be brought to a swift end and that other interventions (ie inserting a catheter) wouldn’t have been suitable / possible due to my history. In the end, I was prepped for a c section but actually just had a spinal and forceps. I feel this significantly reduced trauma, and I felt recognised and well cared for.
<!-- /source-quote -->

<a id="pbu06-0130"></a>
**[pbu06-0130]**

<!-- source-quote:pbu06-0130 -->
> Literally hours after giving birth and finding out my baby actually wasn’t huge and rather a normal size she kept saying how it was a shame I had a C section and not a normal vaginal birth.
<!-- /source-quote -->

<a id="pbu06-0144"></a>
**[pbu06-0144]**

<!-- source-quote:pbu06-0144 -->
> I had a horribly traumatic birth that started with a painful and abrupt induction which culminated in hours of pushing, a 9lb baby who was looking slightly the wrong way, forceps, and a second degree episiotomy. I had to spend 5 unexpected days in the hospital where I felt like no one on the medical staff actually cared much about what happened to me one way or the other (except for whether or not I was breastfeeding. Guess what. Breastfeeding cancelled on account of trauma. Deal with it.) while I scrambled with trying to get the medical team to get me on a treatment plan for new severe urinary incontinence.
<!-- /source-quote -->

<a id="pbu06-0225"></a>
**[pbu06-0225]**

<!-- source-quote:pbu06-0225 -->
> They kept telling me I was a brave girl, that I could do it. It felt like pressure. Bc it was. And it felt, ironically, anti-me. Disrespectful of who I am as a personw my choices, my wants, my dignity. Because it was.
<!-- /source-quote -->

<a id="pbu06-0226"></a>
**[pbu06-0226]**

<!-- source-quote:pbu06-0226 -->
> I threatened to file a complaint. I got the epidural.
> 
> Then they wanted me on my back but I knew I could labor on my side. So I did. They couldn't force me. They rolled their eyes and said I was being ridiculous on my side. Yes, they actually said that.
<!-- /source-quote -->

<a id="pbu29-0129"></a>
**[pbu29-0129]**

<!-- source-quote:pbu29-0129 -->
> I’ve tried therapy a couple times and I never felt understood. It seems like I’m just shouting into a void at this point because no one else seems to agree. 100% definitely a traumatic situation, and I’ll attempt mental health support again, but I feel like it’ll be futile.
<!-- /source-quote -->

<a id="pbu29-0130"></a>
**[pbu29-0130]**

<!-- source-quote:pbu29-0130 -->
> Supposedly they do exist, but I haven’t met one who was equipped to handle my trauma. When I went the maternal mental health route, I was essentially told that I should be happy because we’re both okay, but obviously I’m not okay.
<!-- /source-quote -->

<a id="pbu30-0001"></a>
**[pbu30-0001]**

<!-- source-quote:pbu30-0001 -->
> Before he started the procedure and after I had my epidural placed and was under the influence of narcotics, my OBGYN said to me casually, "By the way I forgot to ask you, what are your plans for the future - do you plan to have more kids? Because I can tie your tubes if you'd like." I was floored by the question I was under the influence of narcotics and I was already strapped at the table and paralyzed. This didn't seem like the right time to ask such a question. I replied emphatically that I did not want him to touch my tubes and that I would like to have more children in the future.
<!-- /source-quote -->

<a id="pbu30-0002"></a>
**[pbu30-0002]**

<!-- source-quote:pbu30-0002 -->
> After the blood pressure crash I started to disassociate. I couldn't deal with what was going on in the room and I felt really confused and alone. Nobody was translating anything for me and nobody was speaking to me throughout the procedure.
<!-- /source-quote -->

<a id="pbu30-0003"></a>
**[pbu30-0003]**

<!-- source-quote:pbu30-0003 -->
> This birth experience has brought up a lot of stuff for me surrounding previous experiences with sexual assault and I have felt like this felt weirdly similar.
> 
> Everyone keeps telling me that all that matters is that the baby is okay and I am okay. I am so sick of hearing that. I am not okay. My husband has been really supportive, but it feels like everyone else gets really uncomfortable when I tell them what happened and just minimize it because they can't handle a few minutes of earnesty or social discomfort.
<!-- /source-quote -->

<a id="pbu30-0007"></a>
**[pbu30-0007]**

<!-- source-quote:pbu30-0007 -->
> Has anybody refused ALL cervical exams before/during an induction? Is there anything else I should know about birthing in a hospital setting that I might find triggering that I should prepare for, either that it will happen or something I can have my husband prepped to help advocate/refuse for me? I'm just very concerned in this setting that the panic that could result would make things worse and end up with need further interventions that I don't want like a c-section
<!-- /source-quote -->

<a id="pbu30-0102"></a>
**[pbu30-0102]**

<!-- source-quote:pbu30-0102 -->
> I had a pretty traumatic birth story. It was the worst day of my life. After so many awful things to bring my baby out of my womb, they took him away without letting me see his face, or touch him and then wouldn’t let me see him for ten hours after. They said it was because I had a c section. Later, I was told they’ve never done that and no one understands why it was done for me. The days following were more close calls for me, mostly due to hospital negligence. CFS leak headache for 4 days. Narrowly escaped cervical hematoma only because I mentioned something to the doctor that the nurse had previously told me didn’t need to be mentioned. Seven needles in the spine over 6 days. It just was endless “We are so sorry, we promise this never happens” from the hospital staff.
<!-- /source-quote -->

<a id="pbu30-0279"></a>
**[pbu30-0279]**

<!-- source-quote:pbu30-0279 -->
> All of the sudden my low risk pregnancy with literally 0 issues the entire time became an emergency c section. 10 people ran into the room talking at once, prepping me, having me sign papers, undressing me, cleaning me, it was SO overwhelming. I was sobbing, scared out of my mind.
> 
> The surgery started without my boyfriend and I was alone and terrified. I had never had a single surgery before and here I was, wide awake, feeling like a purse being rummaged through.
<!-- /source-quote -->

<a id="pbu30-0280"></a>
**[pbu30-0280]**

<!-- source-quote:pbu30-0280 -->
> This caused me to start nodding off, at some point the doctor went “oh no oh no” and I literally thought I was dying. I kept myself awake counting ceiling tiles because I was convinced I was dying if I fell asleep. I could hardly breathe and my heart rate was so low I just kept trying to breathe faster and remember to breathe.
<!-- /source-quote -->

**Also reproduced in earlier patterns:** [pbu06-0093](#pbu06-0093) · [pbu06-0098](#pbu06-0098) · [pbu06-0099](#pbu06-0099) · [pbu06-0109](#pbu06-0109) · [pbu06-0110](#pbu06-0110) · [pbu06-0134](#pbu06-0134) · [pbu06-0135](#pbu06-0135) · [pbu06-0136](#pbu06-0136) · [pbu06-0315](#pbu06-0315) · [pbu06-0316](#pbu06-0316) · [pbu06-0395](#pbu06-0395) · [pbu06-0396](#pbu06-0396) · [pbu29-0030](#pbu29-0030) · [pbu29-0031](#pbu29-0031) · [pbu29-0122](#pbu29-0122) · [pbu29-0126](#pbu29-0126) · [pbu29-0127](#pbu29-0127) · [pbu29-0128](#pbu29-0128) · [pbu29-0132](#pbu29-0132) · [pbu29-0134](#pbu29-0134) · [pbu29-0141](#pbu29-0141) · [pbu29-0142](#pbu29-0142) · [pbu29-0143](#pbu29-0143) · [pbu29-0144](#pbu29-0144) · [pbu29-0227](#pbu29-0227) · [pbu29-0228](#pbu29-0228) · [pbu29-0247](#pbu29-0247) · [pbu29-0248](#pbu29-0248) · [pbu29-0272](#pbu29-0272) · [pbu29-0273](#pbu29-0273) · [pbu29-0274](#pbu29-0274) · [pbu29-0349](#pbu29-0349) · [pbu29-0377](#pbu29-0377) · [pbu30-0113](#pbu30-0113) · [pbu30-0114](#pbu30-0114) · [pbu30-0115](#pbu30-0115) · [pbu30-0116](#pbu30-0116) · [pbu30-0117](#pbu30-0117) · [pbu30-0120](#pbu30-0120) · [pbu30-0121](#pbu30-0121) · [pbu30-0154](#pbu30-0154) · [pbu30-0231](#pbu30-0231) · [pbu30-0232](#pbu30-0232) · [pbu30-0233](#pbu30-0233).

**Further supporting ids:** [pbu06-0027] · [pbu06-0267] · [pbu06-0304] · [pbu06-0317] · [pbu29-0209] · [pbu30-0028] · [pbu30-0086] · [pbu30-0087].

<a id="pattern-14"></a>
## 14. Recovery may mean readmission, incontinence, loss of mobility or persistent symptoms while a newborn still needs care.

**Current findings home:** §§2, 4–6, 9–10; postpartum recovery.

**Assessment:** Confirms and extends the revised first-weeks conclusion.

**Current conclusion / wording to revise or extend:**

<!-- findings-quote -->
> Recovery is not just a supplies checklist
<!-- /findings-quote -->

**What should change:** Keep maternal injury and practical incapacity explicit, including untreated tears, wound care, urinary injury, Bell's palsy, hemorrhage, retained placenta, fistula, rhinitis and pain-management restrictions. Expand the promised two-week horizon when symptoms persist. Retain both easy and difficult recovery accounts.

**Strong and distinctive evidence:**

<a id="pbu06-0068"></a>
**[pbu06-0068]**

<!-- source-quote:pbu06-0068 -->
> I am drowning… I’ve been crying and I’m feeling so depressed I would be back to work but due to complications with my incision I didn’t get cleared by my OB
<!-- /source-quote -->

<a id="pbu06-0074"></a>
**[pbu06-0074]**

<!-- source-quote:pbu06-0074 -->
> Then I started having so much pain when I tried to pee that eventually I literally couldn’t pee. I ended up in the ER on Christmas, three days after my daughter was born. My fiancé couldn’t come in because of COVID restrictions. It turned out I had additional tears that hadn’t been treated when I gave birth. They had to put in a catheter and basically teach me how to cover the tears with my hand and angle my body differently so I could pee without excruciating pain.
<!-- /source-quote -->

<a id="pbu06-0075"></a>
**[pbu06-0075]**

<!-- source-quote:pbu06-0075 -->
> Long story short, she got drunk while he went to the store to get formula. He had to kick her out, and she proceeded to stay in her van in our driveway for like two days, calling me from the driveway crying while I was IN THE HOSPITAL, telling me he was going to call the cops on her, that we hated her, etc.  
> Meanwhile I am trying not to fucking die and can barely even TALK because I don’t have enough oxygen, and I’m getting calls from my fiancé, my mom, my mom’s sister, everybody. It was just a whole fucking thing that made an already terrifying situation so much more stressful.
<!-- /source-quote -->

<a id="pbu06-0086"></a>
**[pbu06-0086]**

<!-- source-quote:pbu06-0086 -->
> My recovery has also been horrible. A nurse made a mistake and did not deflate the catheter when removing, which ripped my urethra. I also tore internally in two places which required stitches, and have hemorrhoids. My urethral tear ended up getting infected and required yet another hospitalization. To say the least, I have been in incredible pain and relied on painkillers. Not to mention I picked up some sort of sickness from the hospital and every cough is agony. I am at my wits end and losing my sanity from how much physical turmoil this entire 11 months has caused me.
<!-- /source-quote -->

<a id="pbu06-0087"></a>
**[pbu06-0087]**

<!-- source-quote:pbu06-0087 -->
> Not to mention it was incredibly expensive because of all the hospital visits and my husband and I having to eat takeout for 9 months because I could no longer cook.
<!-- /source-quote -->

<a id="pbu06-0118"></a>
**[pbu06-0118]**

<!-- source-quote:pbu06-0118 -->
> That was until I woke up the morning after delivery and couldn’t walk. I was apparently the 1 in 300,000 that suffered a rare birth injury that left me paralyzed in one leg. My milk never came in and my dreams of breastfeeding never had a chance because I learned at 8 weeks pp I again had retained placenta and needed an emergency surgery (my 4th lifetime D&C).
<!-- /source-quote -->

<a id="pbu06-0119"></a>
**[pbu06-0119]**

<!-- source-quote:pbu06-0119 -->
> I can’t walk, I can’t carry my baby. I can barely care for myself let alone anyone else. I can’t stop asking why me? I suffered so much for years to get to a healthy pregnancy  while everyone else in my life just had the easiest time. And now yet again, I’m suffering when I should be enjoying my baby and motherhood.
<!-- /source-quote -->

<a id="pbu06-0121"></a>
**[pbu06-0121]**

<!-- source-quote:pbu06-0121 -->
> From 1 week pre-birth to now I have struggled with urinary incontinence to no avail of fixing it. I am on a wait list for further help but who know when that will be!
<!-- /source-quote -->

<a id="pbu06-0145"></a>
**[pbu06-0145]**

<!-- source-quote:pbu06-0145 -->
> I keep wracking my brain with how this could have been prevented. I don't blame my son- I blame the NHS for not being able to prevent this outcome and I blame myself for thinking I could handle being a mother. I'm so scared about my incontinence and all I want is to go back to last year before becoming pregnant.
<!-- /source-quote -->

<a id="pbu06-0305"></a>
**[pbu06-0305]**

<!-- source-quote:pbu06-0305 -->
> My son is perfect. I ended up with an infection after all. It's been a very difficult recovery. Fevers up to 103, but they finally came down in the last 2 days. Hematomas from the top of my belly button to my pelvic bone, below the scar. Daily packing of the wound and draining the blood. Luckily, the infection didn't reach my blood stream or uterus.
<!-- /source-quote -->

<a id="pbu29-0003"></a>
**[pbu29-0003]**

<!-- source-quote:pbu29-0003 -->
> Needless to say, navigating postpartum and bell’s palsy simultaneously was extremely challenging, from a mental and physical health perspective - I couldn’t smile, eat, or drink water properly, and instead of having the time to rest my body, I immediately had to feed/nurse a baby through day and night. This was the hardest part of my postpartum journey, so if you are going through this, I want to send solidarity and reassurance that it gets better. Sharing my fast recovery timeline and tips below. Doctor said to expect recovery to take 6-12 weeks and I was on the early side.
<!-- /source-quote -->

<a id="pbu29-0004"></a>
**[pbu29-0004]**

<!-- source-quote:pbu29-0004 -->
> I was able to heal faster when I got longer stretches of sleep at night and took naps. So lean on your support system to ensure you can get this (e.g. maybe someone gives baby a bottle so you can sleep more).
<!-- /source-quote -->

<a id="pbu29-0005"></a>
**[pbu29-0005]**

<!-- source-quote:pbu29-0005 -->
> I bought eye patches but only used it a few times.
> * I know it’s hard, but try to remind yourself it WILL get better. It's so hard to stay positive in the newborn trenches, so allow yourself to feel all the feelings, but don’t forget to enjoy the time with your baby - you can do this!!
<!-- /source-quote -->

<a id="pbu29-0096"></a>
**[pbu29-0096]**

<!-- source-quote:pbu29-0096 -->
> I gave birth on Thursday and everything was going perfectly fine until Sunday evening when I started feeling short of breath and waking up gasping for air. I panicked after researching that it could be cardiomyopathy and rushed myself to the hospital. During this same time, baby hadn't pooped in 26 hours or peed in close to 8 hours so my fiance and I were scared about that as well. We decided that he should take the baby to ER to get assessed because he was also pretty yellow.
<!-- /source-quote -->

<a id="pbu29-0097"></a>
**[pbu29-0097]**

<!-- source-quote:pbu29-0097 -->
> Obviously being separated from my baby was the last thing I wanted because it was day 3 and we were figuring out my transition milk situation. I ended up being admitted to the hospital and needed to stay overnight so I missed an entire day with my newborn (devastated is an understatement). He was given formula in the meantime and he was perfectly fine after his exam. He went home with my fiance and his parents to care for him overnight.
<!-- /source-quote -->

<a id="pbu29-0098"></a>
**[pbu29-0098]**

<!-- source-quote:pbu29-0098 -->
> I just feel so guilty putting him through 2 days of medical intervention that ended up being pointless, all while being away from him for most of it. I feel like I put him through too much for only being 5 days old and I'm scared I permanently messed up our feeding and put stress on his nervous system 😭
<!-- /source-quote -->

<a id="pbu29-0137"></a>
**[pbu29-0137]**

<!-- source-quote:pbu29-0137 -->
> She didn’t do anything to help my postpartum recovery. I had to be the one to make my own tea, do laundry clean, make my own healthy meals all while I process how the father doesn’t want to be with me and can sleep at night never knowing his daughter.
<!-- /source-quote -->

<a id="pbu29-0139"></a>
**[pbu29-0139]**

<!-- source-quote:pbu29-0139 -->
> Anyways, had a terrible pregnancy due to thyroid issues and some issues that led to c section, also exacerbated by living with toxic MIL and not feeling supported. Factor in having a fragmented family , sister is autistic and has behaviour issues, mum is dealing with that and dad is quite a temperamental man. I’ve always been a middle man for dealing with other people’s problems. But when I need help I get met with “get over it” and “that’s life”.
<!-- /source-quote -->

<a id="pbu29-0324"></a>
**[pbu29-0324]**

<!-- source-quote:pbu29-0324 -->
> One thing that confuses me is that during the first few days after our daughter was born, he was genuinely supportive and helpful. He was kind to me, checked in on me, helped more and I felt like we were in it together. After those first few days, though, things seemed to change. He became much less supportive, much more focused on getting back to his normal routine, and I increasingly felt like I was carrying the practical and emotional load on my own.
<!-- /source-quote -->

<a id="pbu29-0325"></a>
**[pbu29-0325]**

<!-- source-quote:pbu29-0325 -->
> Around a week after our daughter was born, he told me he was feeling overwhelmed because he'd lost his normal routine of going to the gym and socialising. I appreciate becoming a parent is a huge adjustment for both of us, but I found it difficult to hear because I was one week post-C-section, recovering from major surgery, doing all the night feeds and caring for our newborn around the clock. It felt like we were overwhelmed by very different things.
<!-- /source-quote -->

<a id="pbu30-0098"></a>
**[pbu30-0098]**

<!-- source-quote:pbu30-0098 -->
> I stopped working when I had a threatened miscarriage and hyperemesis. It was a mutual decision and we were okay. I gave birth 4 months ago. I had to be admitted 7 days post op for a serious infection for three days. That medical bill added up. Now I got the diagnosis that I have a child birth injury and a fistula which will need a reconstructive surgery. That will be another bill.
<!-- /source-quote -->

<a id="pbu30-0201"></a>
**[pbu30-0201]**

<!-- source-quote:pbu30-0201 -->
> I bought some PP undies that are high waisted and curious when people switched to those instead of the mesh undies. I get freaked out doing anything near or around the incision so I don’t want to do something that could upset it lol
<!-- /source-quote -->
