**Further supporting ids:** [pbu06-0134] · [pbu06-0135] · [pbu06-0136] · [pbu29-0177] · [pbu29-0286] · [pbu29-0287] · [pbu30-0053] · [pbu29-0072].

<a id="pattern-15"></a>
## 15. Feeding decisions involve reliable function, pain, sleep, treatment and bodily boundaries, not only information about benefits.

**Current findings home:** §§2, 4–6, 9–10; feeding and first-weeks sections.

**Assessment:** Extends the first-weeks and feeding-pressure interpretation.

**Current conclusion / wording to revise or extend:**

<!-- findings-quote -->
> The first weeks include physical incapacity, feeding pressure, conflicting instructions, loneliness, regret and delayed connection.
<!-- /findings-quote -->

**What should change:** Include choices not to start, inability to continue, mixed feeding, unreliable latch or shields, pressure from relatives and the way treatment or nighttime care interacts with feeding. Preserve parents who wanted breastfeeding and feel loss alongside parents who value stopping. One successful latch is not the same as a usable feeding plan.

**Strong and distinctive evidence:**

<a id="pbu06-0062"></a>
**[pbu06-0062]**

<!-- source-quote:pbu06-0062 -->
> I breast feed and have already cut out dairy because I noticed early on she struggled when I consumed it. We had her tongue tie released. She struggles with latching but working on it. We have also tried formula and different bottle types, she still  struggles to latch correctly. I spend more time consoling her and trying to get a burp when I use bottles. I believe her issue is rooted around feeding and trapped gas however she is so inconsistent it’s hard to determine why / what. We have switched our bottles and do all the gas drops, techniques etc.
<!-- /source-quote -->

<a id="pbu06-0084"></a>
**[pbu06-0084]**

<!-- source-quote:pbu06-0084 -->
> We struggled swaddling for a while because it stressed her out that she couldn’t put her hands up so we just didn’t swaddle pretty much since birth
<!-- /source-quote -->

<a id="pbu06-0132"></a>
**[pbu06-0132]**

<!-- source-quote:pbu06-0132 -->
> When we came back home we were hosting my mother until she found a house to rent after her divorce, and she imposed greatly on everything I did. She told me that I would suffocate my babies with my breasts when I tried breastfeeding, she would call me irrational when I said I didn't want her interfering with everything all the time, and she would straight up take my babies and not allow me to hug them, whilst shaming me for not managing to bond with them and produce enough milk, and blame their stomach pain on my milk because my food has "spices" in it.
<!-- /source-quote -->

<a id="pbu29-0152"></a>
**[pbu29-0152]**

<!-- source-quote:pbu29-0152 -->
> 1. Baby boy is born via emergency c section after a traumatic back to back labour. I was exhausted and broke down on night two as he was screaming. He was starving and I couldn't stay awake. I opted to give him formula and combi-feed. Felt like a failure. Pumped for 6 weeks, couldn't produce the amount of milk he needed. Started exclusively formula feeding and I know deep down this was the best decision for my 99th percentile baby who is growing out of 9-12 month clothes at 6 months... but I still feel like a failure.
<!-- /source-quote -->

<a id="pbu29-0153"></a>
**[pbu29-0153]**

<!-- source-quote:pbu29-0153 -->
> 2. Baby boy was taking 4oz of milk per feed at 3 days old. I worried it would hurt his tummy and looked on reddit where the main message was that he was being overfed. I was so anxious, turns out he needed it and he was fine.
<!-- /source-quote -->

<a id="pbu29-0251"></a>
**[pbu29-0251]**

<!-- source-quote:pbu29-0251 -->
> With my first, my hormones were everywhere and I ended up with oversupply and “rocks” while I couldn’t feed her in the nicu for a month. After that, the lactation consultant had me die down on pumping to lower supply then it stopped completely and I wasn’t able to breastfeed anymore.  I am currently expecting and looking forward to being able too, is there hope? Should I get excited? Is it possible? I can already feel the milk mamas growing at 8weeks pregnant 😭
<!-- /source-quote -->

<a id="pbu29-0282"></a>
**[pbu29-0282]**

<!-- source-quote:pbu29-0282 -->
> Does choosing not to BF make me bad? I did a bit of combo feeding when my 12m old was a newborn for about 6 weeks because BF really hurt. I went through the guilt already especially how it’s so good for them to avoid sicknesses of when they are sick. Anyways I’m 37 weeks pregnant and I do not want to attempt BF. Nothing triggers me, my 12m old pulling my hair, kicking, biting me etc nothing overstimulates me. But having a hand graze over my nipple I cannot stand, it makes me feel gross I don’t know why like I want to chop off my boobs! Even just from pumping a big 11 months ago, to me they are now not sexual things and are just gross!
<!-- /source-quote -->

<a id="pbu30-0054"></a>
**[pbu30-0054]**

<!-- source-quote:pbu30-0054 -->
> We could have afforded childcare if I was able to keep breastfeeding but sadly I wasn't physically able to. It was a surprise to us.
<!-- /source-quote -->

<a id="pbu30-0111"></a>
**[pbu30-0111]**

<!-- source-quote:pbu30-0111 -->
> By day 5 she was weighed and had lost 9.5% of her birth weight. The midwife scared us saying she was borderline being taken back to hospital. She then gave us some very wrong advice about formula feeding which caused us a lot of unnecessary stress, but we're back on track now feeding correctly and focusing on her weight gain.
<!-- /source-quote -->

<a id="pbu30-0112"></a>
**[pbu30-0112]**

<!-- source-quote:pbu30-0112 -->
> The nipple shields had mixed success rate and I only wanted to breastfeed if I could guarantee a latch each time, not just some of the time, so I gave up trying or even pumping.
<!-- /source-quote -->

<a id="pbu30-0150"></a>
**[pbu30-0150]**

<!-- source-quote:pbu30-0150 -->
> reading more and more about breastfeeding, i do appreciate the information, data, and benefits. but i also value my mental health, and i’ve ultimately decided that to preserve my mental health, i don’t want to breastfeed.
> 
> my mom is a doctor and she is very adamant about breastfeeding. she acts like not breastfeeding is almost abusive, and i’m honestly terrified to tell her that i’ve chosen not to breastfeed.
<!-- /source-quote -->

**Also reproduced in earlier patterns:** [pbu06-0116](#pbu06-0116) · [pbu06-0117](#pbu06-0117) · [pbu06-0118](#pbu06-0118) · [pbu06-0141](#pbu06-0141) · [pbu06-0144](#pbu06-0144) · [pbu06-0266](#pbu06-0266) · [pbu29-0096](#pbu29-0096) · [pbu29-0097](#pbu29-0097) · [pbu29-0098](#pbu29-0098) · [pbu29-0147](#pbu29-0147) · [pbu29-0148](#pbu29-0148) · [pbu30-0037](#pbu30-0037) · [pbu30-0074](#pbu30-0074).

**Further supporting ids:** [pbu29-0091] · [pbu30-0106].

<a id="pattern-16"></a>
## 16. Some partners anticipate and share the work; others withdraw, carry invisible fear or misunderstand how much work exists.

**Current findings home:** §§5–6, 9–10, 17.1, 17.6.

**Assessment:** Confirms the gap and extends the other-side account.

**Current conclusion / wording to revise or extend:**

<!-- findings-quote -->
> From his side, he wants to help and doesn't know how
<!-- /findings-quote -->

**What should change:** Keep supportive partners, worried partners, practical neglect and abuse analytically separate. Include his hidden fear after resuscitation, his employment pressure, a father recalculating his real share, disability-related planning and a parent's choice to exclude an unsafe partner from the birth. A parent's grief may differ from the partner's without either representing all fathers.

**Strong and distinctive evidence:**

<a id="pbu06-0015"></a>
**[pbu06-0015]**

<!-- source-quote:pbu06-0015 -->
> Then he tells me “you talk about miscarriages so much it’s like you want to have one”.
<!-- /source-quote -->

<a id="pbu06-0040"></a>
**[pbu06-0040]**

<!-- source-quote:pbu06-0040 -->
> I’m trying to prepare to become a mom while grieving my relationship at the same time. It’s incredibly lonely. I miss him every day, and it’s hard not to wonder if he’s moving on while my entire life has changed overnight.
<!-- /source-quote -->

<a id="pbu06-0041"></a>
**[pbu06-0041]**

<!-- source-quote:pbu06-0041 -->

<a id="pattern-17"></a>
## 17. Family help can become surveillance, loss of privacy, control of the baby or a debt that appears to buy a vote.

**Current findings home:** §§3, 5–6, 8–10, 16.1, 17.4.

**Assessment:** Extends; qualifies an ordinary visitor-and-helper plan.

**Current conclusion / wording to revise or extend:**

<!-- findings-quote -->
> A visitor and helper plan written in the third trimester is supported.
<!-- /findings-quote -->

**What should change:** Explain help by its effect on the parent: respect, recovery, retained agency and ability to refuse. Include relatives' disclosure of loss or complications, announcements taken over, judgment about sex or marital status, fear of family blowups, abuse and gifts used as leverage. Boundaries may need attention early in pregnancy. Include helpful family members and cultural limits on sending a helper away.

**Strong and distinctive evidence:**

<a id="pbu06-0009"></a>
**[pbu06-0009]**

<!-- source-quote:pbu06-0009 -->
> She even said, in front of my partner, that she won’t help with the baby if we’re not married first. It really hurt him, especially because he’s always been kind to her.
<!-- /source-quote -->

<a id="pbu06-0088"></a>
**[pbu06-0088]**

<!-- source-quote:pbu06-0088 -->

> You won’t be happy with just one.   
> You’ll end up spoiling your one child.   
> Don’t worry about the finances.

I told them that my husband would not be home and that my mother Mother was coming one of the days to help with stuff. Of course they didn't wanna come when my mother was there and decided to cancel it altogether.

<a id="pattern-18"></a>
## 18. Income, leave, childcare and insurance can determine whether pregnancy, treatment or leaving a relationship feels possible.

**Current findings home:** §§2, 8–10, 16.2, 17.2.

**Assessment:** Extends the care-cost conclusion beyond delivery choices.

**Current conclusion / wording to revise or extend:**

<!-- findings-quote -->
> Money can change a birth decision.
<!-- /findings-quote -->

**What should change:** Add costs before the pregnancy decision, insurance and contraception cliffs, paid reassurance that cannot be repeated, cancelled showers during admission, education and wedding commitments, dependence on unsafe households and financial obstacles to legal remedies. Treat all benefits and legal statements as authors' understandings. These are not prices for a Mamivio guide.

**Strong and distinctive evidence:**

<a id="pattern-19"></a>
## 19. Personalization must include disability, language, transport, bereavement, housing and the ability to act before a complication is documented.

**Current findings home:** §§5–6, 9–10, 17.2–17.4.

**Assessment:** Extends the situation-selector conclusion.

**Current conclusion / wording to revise or extend:**

<!-- findings-quote -->
> Personalisation by pregnancy number alone is a thin selector.
<!-- /findings-quote -->

**What should change:** Add missing ID, rural travel cost, inability to drive, stairs, arm disability, a partner's seizures, migration, loss of a co-parent, digital-only services and incompatible referral pathways. An option described online may not exist locally. A parent's housing plan can be blocked precisely because she is trying to arrange it before injury occurs.

**Strong and distinctive evidence:**

**[pbu06-0079]**

<!-- source-quote:pbu06-0079 -->

<a id="pattern-20"></a>
## 20. Parents ask for movement, rest and intimacy that feel comfortable and safe.

**Current findings home:** §§2, 4, 6, 9–10.

**Assessment:** Confirms the revised exercise reading and extends it with explicit planning requests.

**Current conclusion / wording to revise or extend:**

<!-- findings-quote -->
> The exercise evidence also includes symptom management and safety questions, correcting the identity-only reading.
<!-- /findings-quote -->

**What should change:** Keep the author seeking a prenatal exercise plan, walking that worsens pain, swimming and walks that help, daily-life lifting, massage and sexual pleasure or fear. Do not collapse bodily autonomy, safe participation, fitness goals and symptom control into one motivation.

**Strong and distinctive evidence:**

<a id="pbu06-0008"></a>
**[pbu06-0008]**

<!-- source-quote:pbu06-0008 -->
> I think I feel confused about what I should and shouldn't do during pregnancy. Anyone have a plan they follow?
<!-- /source-quote -->

**[pbu06-0349]**

<!-- source-quote:pbu06-0349 -->

<!-- /source-quote -->

**[pbu06-0372]**

<!-- source-quote:pbu06-0372 -->

**[pbu06-0378]**

<!-- source-quote:pbu06-0378 -->

**[pbu06-0388]**

<!-- /source-quote -->

All I can think of is getting fucked non stop, all day long. Grrrrr…the brain fog is real. I travel a lot for work so I’m not always home with my husband and I am so frustrated and on edge constantly.
<!-- /source-quote -->

<a id="pbu29-0264"></a>
**[pbu29-0264]**

<!-- source-quote:pbu29-0264 -->
> Today I was walking my dog, and a thunderstorm was rolling in and I heard a distant alarm in the background which freaked me out. I didn’t know if it was a tornado alarm or if the thunderstorm was coming in quicker than I thought so I made a run for it to get closer to my house. I only ran for about 20 seconds and then my heart rate got really high, and I felt super out of breath. I’m pretty in shape and played a lot of sports prior to this pregnancy so I wasn’t expecting to get that tired from a quick run like that. I think on top of that I was scared of the alarm so my heart rate spiked a little more because of that. Anyway, I got home and I started having a weird pain in my side that lasted for about five minutes, now I’m here typing this up. Can a sharp raise in heart rate lead to a miscarriage?
<!-- /source-quote -->

<a id="pbu29-0284"></a>
**[pbu29-0284]**

<!-- source-quote:pbu29-0284 -->

<!-- /source-quote -->

I *know* it’s not a real baby bump because I’d go to sleep looking like a beach ball and wake up almost flat kkkk.  
> Today, after a massive IBS diarrhea episode and releasing a ton of gas, my belly finally deflated. No bloat, no trapped stool, no bump... but now my belly feels like a half-empty water balloon with my intestines just dancing around inside 😂. And worse: I can literally feel my abdominal muscles separating right now.  
> I’m terrified that being constantly blown up like a pufferfish for weeks is going to give me severe diastasis recti.  
> Has anyone with IBS dealt with this during early pregnancy? Is there anything safe I can do right now (breathing techniques, pelvic floor / core support) to protect my abs while dealing with the constant bloating?
<!-- /source-quote -->

<a id="pbu29-0322"></a>
**[pbu29-0322]**

<!-- source-quote:pbu29-0322 -->

<!-- /source-quote -->

<a id="pattern-21"></a>
## 21. Parents buy practical relief, scans and human help, but a purchase can create a new problem and does not demonstrate demand for this guide.

**Current findings home:** §§4, 6, 8, 10–11, 17.4–17.6.

**Assessment:** Confirms adjacent purchases and extends what value means.

**Current conclusion / wording to revise or extend:**

<!-- findings-quote -->
> There is no direct buyer evidence for the proposed guide.
<!-- /findings-quote -->

**What should change:** Include whether the purchase is usable: safe caregiver behavior, crib/mattress fit, uncertain refunds, reliable latch, relief without next-day sedation and a care service that actually explains. Some call paid support worthwhile; others cannot trust or afford it. Preserve promotional and AI-assisted provenance rather than treating these as independent product efficacy evidence.

**Strong and distinctive evidence:**

<a id="pbu06-0194"></a>
**[pbu06-0194]**

<!-- source-quote:pbu06-0194 -->
> I tracked my BBT very closely with my oura ring and natural cycles.
<!-- /source-quote -->

<!-- /source-quote -->

> so I took 2 pregnancy test both came back positive bought 2 more the next day 2 positive 1 negative and bought 2 more both negative so I ended up making an appointment with my doctor for April 2nd for a pregnancy test and a full std panel came back negative for both but I still was feeling off a day later I started have weird cramps in my upper stomach and vagina looked it up they said it could just be pms I never had pms I never and I mean never but body changes so didn’t think to much of  until the pains got really bad so I took maybe 5 more test 2 positive 3 negative  I ended up with more weird symptoms extremely tired ,breast tenderness my nipples hurted so bad felt like they were on fire random stomach pains in my side upper stomach lower stomach vagina headaches still was thinking pms my period ended up coming again now this period was super weird I didn’t start a day early which was kinda weird cause I always start early but didn’t think too much about it but my first day was so bad really really bad crampss super heavy bleed and I’m not a heavy bleeder at all and so many clots like so many a lot large I end up taking a test my first day and I had a very faint positive  my second day even worse so much blood and so many cramps now my third day was weird start regular flow then pink for a couple hours then back regular flow today no blood at all which is weird cause I still have 1 day and even when my period ends I still spot a little bit of spotting not just nothing so I really don’t think I’m pregnant at all I’m not to concerned about that and if by some chance I am then that’s crazy but my question is did the plan b mess my period up like is this why it’s like this cause today minus the no blood I’m have all these weird cramps very where still and the headaches will my periods go back normal after this and will I have to deal with this longer all answer are really appreciate
<!-- /source-quote -->

I'm mostly worried cause I've literally never had a positive test before, and I've been trying for two years. How likely is a false positive? I have had a cold for about 4 days now and wonder if im actually sick or if its pregnancy? What should I do next as im 0- blood and ive heard that my body can attack the baby is that true. Any and all advice appreciated. I'm just nervous.

**[pbu30-0211]**

<!-- source-quote:pbu30-0211 -->
> When I was pregnant with my daughter, I never missed a day of my prenatal vitamins. I was obsessed with finding the best supplements through scientific research. I was diagnosed with moderate learning disabilities at 19, and i was sick a lot as a kid. I wanted to do anything I could to  give her the best chance at not facing the same struggles. She is almost 2 now. She continues to amaze me with her advanced language skills, curiosity, memory, and intellect. These things are a mix of so many factors, but I wanted to share my routine as well as my research, in case anyone is interested in following the same routine.
<!-- /source-quote -->

<a id="pbu30-0212"></a>
**[pbu30-0212]**

<!-- source-quote:pbu30-0212 -->
> **My routine:**
> 
> • Folate (400–800 mcg daily) neural tube and nervous system development.  
> • Iron (27 mg daily) supports oxygen delivery and healthy brain development.  
> • Iodine (220 mcg daily) thyroid hormones and cognitive development.  
> • Vitamin D (2,000 IU daily) immune function and bone development.  
> • Magnesium (360 mg daily) nerve, muscle, and cellular function.  
> • Choline (450 mg daily) brain development, memory formation, and healthy cell membranes.  
> • DHA (300 mg daily) develops brain and retina.
<!-- /source-quote -->

<a id="pbu06-0336"></a>
**[pbu06-0336]**

<!-- source-quote:pbu06-0336 -->
> The pediatrician told me that she believes that the vaccine saved her from a severe illness, since RSV is brutal on babies, let alone those who are a few days old. I am just so grateful that I had the option, and access, to a vaccine that ended up protecting my baby so thoroughly.
<!-- /source-quote -->

<a id="pbu29-0368"></a>
**[pbu29-0368]**

<!-- source-quote:pbu29-0368 -->
> I had a very scary week. I am 14 weeks pregnant and on Sunday I suddenly had an incident of heavy bleeding. I rushed to my hospital and my doctor immediately gave me an ultrasound and found pregnancy to be safe and the baby very healthy and active. I was so relieved. Doctor couldn't find any reason for the bleed and was so confused. I am APLA positive so I was on heparin and ecospirin. She immediately stopped my blood thinners and started Traxenamic acid in IV for three days. But just after day one of IV...my bleeding stopped and the issue resolved. Doctor checked my vagina and said there was no issue with the cervix or vagina too. So urine culture was taken. UTI was the culprit. Thankfully it was not too severe and my kidneys were alright. The doctor said UTI could've caused inflammation in my vagina and my blood thinners would've worsened it. I was immediately given three day antibiotics via IV and my condition improved. Now, augmentin has been prescribed along with cranberry juice for five days. It's been such a rollercoaster week for me. From crying hard to being so happy seeing my baby so healthy and active...uffff!!!
<!-- /source-quote -->

<a id="pattern-22"></a>
## 22. Some parents ask for the real picture, others for positive stories, and some explicitly want both.

**Current findings home:** §§3–4, 6–7, 10–11, 16.4, 17.6.

**Assessment:** Confirms and sharpens the conflicting information preferences.

**Current conclusion / wording to revise or extend:**

<!-- findings-quote -->
> some people seek direct information and others seek less.
<!-- /findings-quote -->

**What should change:** Do not choose one editorial preference for the whole audience. Preserve requests to avoid loss stories, requests for positive and negative outcomes, frustration when experience-seeking is moderated away, and authors who disclaim prediction. Useful help can be a specific breathing exercise, appointment script, list for the OB or peer presence while waiting, rather than another general information product.

**Strong and distinctive evidence:**

<a id="pbu06-0006"></a>
**[pbu06-0006]**

<!-- source-quote:pbu06-0006 -->
> I’m not looking for “it’s all beautiful” answers, I really want the real picture.
<!-- /source-quote -->

<a id="pbu06-0139"></a>
**[pbu06-0139]**

<!-- source-quote:pbu06-0139 -->
> I’d really appreciate hearing both positive and negative experiences. I’m not looking for reassurance only — I’d genuinely like to understand what others have experienced.
<!-- /source-quote -->

<a id="pbu06-0373"></a>
**[pbu06-0373]**

<!-- source-quote:pbu06-0373 -->
> Looking for any similar stories? Positive or otherwise to be honest, I’m a little lost.
<!-- /source-quote -->

<a id="pbu29-0125"></a>
**[pbu29-0125]**

<!-- source-quote:pbu29-0125 -->
> I’d really appreciate hearing all experiences, including pregnancies that progressed normally and those that unfortunately didn’t.**  
> Did you experience red bleeding, cramps or small clots? How long did your symptoms last, and what was your eventual outcome?
<!-- /source-quote -->

<a id="pbu30-0094"></a>
**[pbu30-0094]**

<!-- source-quote:pbu30-0094 -->
> I'd love to hear from the ladies who had smooth, uneventful pregnancies. How did you get through the early weeks, and what helped with the worry?
> 
> Thank you, it would mean a lot to hear some good stories. 💛
<!-- /source-quote -->

<a id="pbu30-0128"></a>
**[pbu30-0128]**

<!-- source-quote:pbu30-0128 -->
> So please share if you’ve had a similar experience to me and what this meant for you. Whether it’s a story of hope or a miscarriage, I can be more prepared for any outcome tomorrow. I also know every woman is different and someone else’s experience doesn’t equal mine, it just might help me whilst I’m going crazy waiting for tomorrow and overthinking everything.
<!-- /source-quote -->

<a id="pbu30-0131"></a>
**[pbu30-0131]**

<!-- source-quote:pbu30-0131 -->
> I’m not looking for false reassurance just genuinely want to know if there are any success out there, because that’s what I’m trying to hold onto right now.
<!-- /source-quote -->

<a id="pbu30-0199"></a>
**[pbu30-0199]**

<!-- source-quote:pbu30-0199 -->
> Obviously my head is absolutely everywhere, there is so much unknown and while pregnancy feels uncontrollable already…nothing feels promising, there’s so much uncertainty…gray…and loss or heartbreak feels inevitable. I feel so disconnected from this pregnancy…I left the US pics in the car bc I just feel so hopeless and detatched. Nothing like my experience with my son.
> 
> Does anyone have any experience with anything like this at all, good/bad/ugly. Just trying to truly understand outcomes since all I can really do is sit here and wait.
<!-- /source-quote -->

<a id="pbu30-0321"></a>
**[pbu30-0321]**

<!-- source-quote:pbu30-0321 -->
> Now, it seems like all my pregnancy symptoms have disappeared. Have I had a miscarriage? My doctor wants me to go for bloodwork first thing tomorrow morning, but I’m so scared that I won’t even be able to sleep tonight. I am so, so scared
> 
> I would really appreciate some honest experiences—both positive and negative. ❤️
<!-- /source-quote -->

## Outliers and counter-examples

These passages qualify the patterns above; they do not establish how often any outcome happens. They also prevent the rewrite from treating all support, intervention, later pregnancies, abortion decisions or information preferences as alike.

### 1. A frightening event and a positive overall birth judgment coexist.

This confirms the revised findings and cuts against a single success/failure label: transfer, intervention and crying do not tell us how the author ultimately names the experience. The UR06 induction story also mentions a promoted device and Gemini in its provenance; it is one account, not independent proof of a device's effect.

**[pbu06-0035]**

<!-- source-quote:pbu06-0035 -->
> Our baby's Apgar score was 7/10. They were bluish and silent. At around two minutes old, they began making little whimpering sounds. At first we thought perhaps the placenta was still supplying enough oxygen, so there simply wasn't much urgency to fully "arrive" in the world yet.
> 
> The placenta was born within ten minutes. Our baby gradually pinked up, but still wasn't breathing consistently. The pulse oximeter showed oxygen levels below the desired range, and later they remained too low even with supplemental oxygen.
<!-- /source-quote -->

**[pbu06-0036]**

<!-- source-quote:pbu06-0036 -->
> At that point, the midwives decided to call an ambulance. We didn't panic because they stayed remarkably calm throughout. They reassured us there was no immediate danger to our baby's life, but that they should be assessed in the hospital.
<!-- /source-quote -->

**[pbu06-0038]**

<!-- source-quote:pbu06-0038 -->
> Looking back, we would absolutely make the same choice again. I'm so grateful that we chose this path. Most of all, I'm deeply thankful to our midwives, who guided us through every step with such calmness, kindness, and confidence. They even came to visit us again on Sunday, just to see how we were doing. ❤️
<!-- /source-quote -->

**[pbu06-0098]**

<!-- source-quote:pbu06-0098 -->
> I felt completely informed and gave 100% consent. By the time it was actively used in the delivery room, the environment became very hectic, and the physical sensation was one of incredibly intense pressure. The pushing and pulling wasn’t comfortable (and my poor husband found it quite distressing to watch visually!), but because the team coached me through exactly what to expect, I felt safe and empowered.
<!-- /source-quote -->

**[pbu06-0099]**

<!-- source-quote:pbu06-0099 -->
> Jokes aside, my labour was incredibly traumatic, long and intensive and I spent the first week crying about how hard it was. I was extremely blessed to deliver with a very strong and capable female consultant and the most amazing midwives who empowered me and aided in making it as positive as possible.
<!-- /source-quote -->

**[pbu06-0136]**

<!-- source-quote:pbu06-0136 -->
> Overall, i’m still very happy that I gave birth to a healthy baby boy and that the medical staff has treated me with such compassion and patience.
<!-- /source-quote -->

### 2. A feared birth can turn out calmer than expected.

These are concrete counterweights to narratives in which the worst expected outcome dominates preparation. They do not establish what will happen to the next reader.

**[pbu30-0058]**

<!-- source-quote:pbu30-0058 -->
> I did a class and a walk through of the hospital but they didn’t go over any breathing exercises. I’m really scared about having a panic attack while I’m at the hospital and giving birth
<!-- /source-quote -->

**[pbu30-0059]**

<!-- source-quote:pbu30-0059 -->
> I just gave birth ON my due date right after this message.. so crazy. You were totally right and I just asked for the anxiety meds which calmed the initial shock when I got there, once it started to really hurt I got the epidural def not that bad and then soon I was napping!! It was not that bad at all and worth every second. Thank you!!!
<!-- /source-quote -->

**[pbu30-0262]**

<!-- source-quote:pbu30-0262 -->
> I had a scheduled c section. Instead of an epidural I got a spinal block.  Sadly that took almost an hour to place. Good news is that was the must painful part.  Once numb I only felt pressure & touches. In about 5 minutes from the cut my daughter was out! She was loud & ready to announce herself. Dad cut the cord & held her as they sewed me up.  
> We stayed in the hospital for 4 days while we figured some health stuff out with me. ( bradycardia ) 
> We are home now,  she is perfect.  My pregnancy sucked but I now see it was all worth it.
<!-- /source-quote -->

**[pbu30-0015]**

<!-- source-quote:pbu30-0015 -->
> My first baby is going to be 11 months soon. Pregnancy and labor and postpartum went overall well, she’s a great kid, we’re enjoying parenthood.
<!-- /source-quote -->

### 3. Paid help and a highly involved partner can be worthwhile.

Keep the hired doula and shared nights beside disappointing support purchases and absent partners. The mother explicitly calls the arrangement a privilege.

**[pbu30-0037]**

<!-- source-quote:pbu30-0037 -->
> A birth doula - I wasn’t sure what a doula even entailed but I went with my gut and hired one early in my pregnancy and I’m so glad I did! She was such a good source of support in late third trimester and during/after birth. It can be so stressful when you’re trying to figure out if you should induce or wait (especially when you’re 35+ and going past 40w) and she helped talk me through it. Having her there for the start of my labor and through birth was incredibly comforting. She knew exactly what to do at each stage, how to advocate for me, and how to coach me through the difficult moments. Pretty sure her latching my baby right after birth was a big reason breastfeeding went so smoothly too! Highly recommend a doula if you can - I found it affordable and well worth it.
<!-- /source-quote -->

**[pbu30-0038]**

<!-- source-quote:pbu30-0038 -->
> Having a husband who takes on >50% of the baby duties in the early days - my husband has fully taken the night shift for the past 8 weeks (I only wake up to pump) and it has allowed me to fully recover physically and be emotionally stable. He also wears the baby for naps as often as he can and is generally extremely involved and present. It’s definitely a privilege to have this setup and has made me realize how much of a difference an involved husband makes in a woman’s postpartum experience.
<!-- /source-quote -->

**[pbu29-0086]**

<!-- source-quote:pbu29-0086 -->
> new mom to a 2 week old. we hired a night nanny/doula via an agency for some nights for the first month and we’re about halfway through our planned days. for most of it it’s been great and really helpful for our sleep.
<!-- /source-quote -->

**[pbu29-0291]**

<!-- source-quote:pbu29-0291 -->
> My husband's been super supportive.  He's been checking in with me how I'm doing both physically and emotionally, helped me find an OB, got groceries for my new nutrition needs, and has been picturing life with our baby and suggested getting a dog.
<!-- /source-quote -->

**[pbu06-0147]**

<!-- source-quote:pbu06-0147 -->
> When I'm not at the hospital or working, I'm meal prepping as many postpartum meals in our instant pot as I can manage. I'm installing car seats. I'm setting up the bassinet, the diaper changing station, the breastfeeding care packages. And I'm preparing for every eventuality I can think of with our 36-week preemie who has been actively practicing  gymnastics and martial arts on my wife's internal organs - whom I will finally get to meet face-to-face in less than 3 weeks.
<!-- /source-quote -->

**[pbu29-0160]**

<!-- source-quote:pbu29-0160 -->
> In reality, what I was seeing and doing "half" of was less than 50% of what we had to do. So i ended up doing at most 25% while she's doing 75%. The problem is that due to how we were raised she had been conditioned to silently do this and build resentment instead of laying it out for me, and I had been conditioned to never once really think about all of this "invisible work."
<!-- /source-quote -->

### 4. Even excellent birth, recovery and sleep support do not guarantee mental well-being.

These accounts cut against attributing postpartum distress only to trauma, weak preparation or an absent helper. Sleep opportunity and the ability to sleep are different.

**[pbu29-0135]**

<!-- source-quote:pbu29-0135 -->
> My labor and birth experience was about as perfect as I could have hoped. I had to be induced at 38 weeks due to a sudden onset of preeclampsia, but the whole process was great. I got my epidural early, I never even felt my contractions, and I slept through all 18 hours of my labor. I pushed nine times across 27 minutes and it was completely painless, I only got one small first degree tear. I feel like I couldn't have hoped for anything better. Even my recovery has been perfect. I had minor cramps that were treated with midol for two days and only bled for a week and a half.
<!-- /source-quote -->

**[pbu29-0136]**

<!-- source-quote:pbu29-0136 -->
> But even with all of that, I still cried all day every day for the first week of his life, and last night I broke down crying with him in my arms and told my husband I didn't think I could live any longer. Now I have an appointment with my OBGYN first thing Monday to get some help, but it all feels so stupid and dramatic. I got everything I wanted and I'm still depressed, how is that possible? I feel so ungrateful and like I'm traumatizing my own baby because he's had to witness all of the break downs.
<!-- /source-quote -->

**[pbu29-0147]**

<!-- source-quote:pbu29-0147 -->
> I’m 8 wks post partum and am having increasing difficulty sleeping. I have moved to a room by myself. My husband and baby are in another room. He’s been taking the night shift to feed the baby essentially leaving me to sleep from about 11:30PM to 7 AM. I do the last dream feed of the night around 10:30PM. Also, I’m completely unable to daytime nap.   
> I pump during daytime only (never did it at night) and I stopped breastfeeding around week 2. I like sleep too much to spend hours awake at night pumping, breastfeeding and then bottle feeding. So nothing should be holding me back from sleeping, right ?
<!-- /source-quote -->

**[pbu29-0148]**

<!-- source-quote:pbu29-0148 -->
> Wrong. I feel exhausted but every time I start to fall into sleep my body wakes me up, all through the night. I’ve tried all the tricks like doing a quiet activity if not asleep by 20 mins, cockroach stretch, not trying to psych myself out with thoughts of not sleeping. I even got prescribed Sertraline (Zoloft) by my Ob for mild PP anxiety but one dose had me feeling AWFUL side effects all day and night including the absolute worst inability to sleep so I did not continue to take it. I’m also taking magnesium in addition to my prenatal and drink a sleepy time chamomile and valerian root tea in the evening. I’ve only resorted to Unisom a handful of times because I don’t want long term dependency or to transfer it in breastmilk. I am desperate to sleep. Any advice?
<!-- /source-quote -->

**[pbu30-0108]**

<!-- source-quote:pbu30-0108 -->
> Her birth was beautiful, easy, and quick. Everything went great stay at the hospital was fabulous!
> 
> However shortly after I got home, everything changed, my anxiety went up, depression got horrible. Developed serve insomnia-  I was blacking out instead of going to sleep. I remember one time finding my baby on the couch stuck crying from losing consciousness. It was 2 weeks of hell, I was suicidal, wasn’t eating or sleeping. I felt in a daze-crying every morning. 
> 
> I ended up in the psychward for over 2 weeks, it helped me I got better I’m on medication for life now, and sleeping pills for my insomnia. I’m finally back to 80% of myself.
<!-- /source-quote -->

### 5. Another postpartum can be easier, and feelings toward the baby can change.

Keep improvement without promising its timing or cause. The update after the regretful second-baby account belongs with the original account, not in a separate success tally.

**[pbu06-0060]**

<!-- source-quote:pbu06-0060 -->
> I would rather go back to work than stay home with my own newborn. I feel awful. Sometimes I just cry all day with her. I’m miserable and I feel like I lost 9 months with my son when I was pregnant because I was so tired all of the time and now I’m even worse off.
<!-- /source-quote -->

**[pbu06-0063]**

<!-- source-quote:pbu06-0063 -->
> ❗️❗️NEW UPDATE- thank you for the good vibes, prayers, and well wishes. I have no idea what happened but something clicked and I no longer feel this way towards my baby. She is sleeping all of the sudden (wakes up at 3 for a bottle) and I have found myself in love with her even on the rough days.
<!-- /source-quote -->

**[pbu06-0265]**

<!-- source-quote:pbu06-0265 -->
> They have varied in terms of my age/hormones, season (I have seasonal affective disorder so my winter baby was rough), medicated vs not (Zoloft has been game changer for prenatal depression & PPA), and the baby themselves!
<!-- /source-quote -->

**[pbu06-0266]**

<!-- source-quote:pbu06-0266 -->
> I was so, so nervous about PPA, the hormone crash, her breastfeeding, etc. I didn't produce enough milk with my last daughter and this time, I'm overproducing. Even if I didn't, we'd have supplemented like before.
<!-- /source-quote -->

**[pbu30-0104]**

<!-- source-quote:pbu30-0104 -->
> I just had my second baby and I’m suddenly realizing my “first time mom” stress was actually just raging, untreated postpartum anxiety. And now I feel really sad for that last version of myself.
> 
> So in case anyone is a first time mom and needs to hear this: It’s apparently NOT normal to constantly fear that you or your baby are going to die, to cry hysterically on a daily basis, to feel like your body is on fire every time your baby cries, or to feel like a negligent abusive mother just for putting your baby down. You’re not a sucky mom and you’re not “just hormonal”! Just FYI!!! Who knew!!! (Not me!)
<!-- /source-quote -->

### 6. A plan can remain useful during hemorrhage or other changed circumstances.

The second-birth account names preparation, sensory tools, an escalation explanation and follow-through. It contrasts with repeated failed therapy and care that treats survival as enough.

**[pbu29-0141]**

<!-- source-quote:pbu29-0141 -->
> EMDR was the biggest help for me during the fourth trimester. It was like night and day before and after my first session, and slowly I was able to start talking about the birth without crying. Over time, I moved from weekly appointments to monthly as a check-in and have kept this up for 2 years now. I see a mental health OT that specialises in Matrescence and Motherhood. A big part of this journey has also been looking at how I see motherhood as a result of my childhood, and doing work there.
<!-- /source-quote -->

**[pbu29-0142]**

<!-- source-quote:pbu29-0142 -->
> When I found out I was pregnant, my OT helped me articulate my beliefs and feelings so I could communicate them to my midwife. Sensory regulation therapy helped me to work out what leads me to feeling overwhelmed/overstimulated, and my biggest triggers (or "smallest cups") are sound and touch. I had started using earplugs for sleep just after the fourth trimester, but have continued every night since and it has improved my quality of sleep so much. My OT helped me put together a "connection kit" of tools/resources I can use when I start feeling overwhelmed or touched out. I took this kit with me to the hospital.
<!-- /source-quote -->

**[pbu29-0143]**

<!-- source-quote:pbu29-0143 -->
> My baby was healthy and I had a grade 1 tear with no bleeding, which we were all very excited about up until I started hemorrhaging from my uterus. My midwife had been prepared for this and there was a clear plan in place. She told my husband to press the call button, the room filled with people and the PPH trolley, and I was given a rundown of what would happen: if they got it under control, great, at 1L of blood loss I would go to theatre, and if it continued I would be looking at a hysterectomy. They got the bleed under control just past 900ML.
<!-- /source-quote -->

**[pbu29-0144]**

<!-- source-quote:pbu29-0144 -->
> While the birth didn't go to plan because I hadn't wanted an induction nor epidural, and I still had a PPH, I am generally very happy with how things went. It was the birth I needed and feels very cathartic after all of the work, research and reading I did put in. The team of doctors and nurses who looked after me were incredible, and I have 6 weeks of home visits now with my midwife for support.
<!-- /source-quote -->

**[pbu29-0129]**

<!-- source-quote:pbu29-0129 -->
> I’ve tried therapy a couple times and I never felt understood. It seems like I’m just shouting into a void at this point because no one else seems to agree. 100% definitely a traumatic situation, and I’ll attempt mental health support again, but I feel like it’ll be futile.
<!-- /source-quote -->

**[pbu29-0130]**

<!-- source-quote:pbu29-0130 -->
> Supposedly they do exist, but I haven’t met one who was equipped to handle my trauma. When I went the maternal mental health route, I was essentially told that I should be happy because we’re both okay, but obviously I’m not okay.
<!-- /source-quote -->

### 7. Clinicians can listen, act promptly and give effective relief.

The positive care scenes sit beside dismissal. They show what the authors found helpful, without validating a diagnosis or treatment effect independently.

**[pbu06-0014]**

<!-- source-quote:pbu06-0014 -->
> My doctor also let me talk through both ends of things and she was really good at acknowledging and being reassuring without making me feel bad.
<!-- /source-quote -->

**[pbu06-0045]**

<!-- source-quote:pbu06-0045 -->
> Anyways- yesterday I went into my appt, the nurses were amazing at explaining what to expect and reassuring me I would be ok. I had the test dose without even realizing and then the full dose shortly after. It was about 2 hours, but no reactions!
<!-- /source-quote -->

**[pbu06-0296]**

<!-- source-quote:pbu06-0296 -->
> I finally get a MFM consult and MFM confirmed that it definitely was fibroid degeneration and they put me on a short course of ibuprofen for 3 days and monitored me and baby and we were fine and discharged. I was able to walk. I wasn’t in any pain when I left the hospital.
<!-- /source-quote -->

**[pbu29-0287]**

<!-- source-quote:pbu29-0287 -->
> At this time there were all new staff and they were all so empathetic, and doing everything in their power to make me comfortable. I was literally crying for hours because of pain.   
> They did bloodwork and saw I indeed had infection. They looked at my cyst and instantly called my OB.   
> He came in on his day off to check me out. His first words when he saw was “oh sweetie I am so sorry”.   
> He admitted me to L&D for some antibiotics and scheduled me for surgery (which happened today)   
> Mind you I have been in so much pain I haven’t been able to pee of over 10 hours, walk or sleep. They had me on antibiotics I ended up having an allergic reaction to and pain meds that terrified me because I am pregnant and all I wanted out of all of this is for my baby to be okay.   
> They finally got me back for surgery. When I woke up I was choking on my own spit and fluid. They took me back to recovery and I was in terrible pain. I had about 6 different nurses surrounding me telling me I was doing great despite how much I was screaming in pain and fear.   
> When I got back to my room I started feeling better with medication and my amazing boyfriend who’s been the best support person throughout this whole thing. I was also constantly reassured my baby was okay…
<!-- /source-quote -->

**[pbu30-0226]**

<!-- source-quote:pbu30-0226 -->

I am already under specialised mental health teams, been referred to all the specialists for the physical needs and I will have a team behind me at every step, which does help. My husband helps where he can, but he works a high stress job and shift work, so there are often days at a time where I will be doing this alone. I also work full time in healthcare. Rest, although advised, is Gold dust for me.
<!-- /source-quote -->

### 8. Certainty about abortion and distress about being pregnant can coexist.

This directly cuts against interpreting every frightened or sad abortion-related passage as ambivalence or regret. Regret and grief also exist and remain separate accounts.

**[pbu29-0372]**

<!-- source-quote:pbu29-0372 -->
> I'm having an abortion next week and I am **100% certain that is what I want**. I genuinely cannot afford a child, I'm nowhere near financially or emotionally ready, and this is completely outside the life plan I've always had for myself.
> 
> I don't want to be pregnant. I don't want a baby right now. **I'm not secretly hoping things were different and I'm not conflicted about the decision to have an abortion.**
<!-- /source-quote -->

**[pbu29-0373]**

<!-- source-quote:pbu29-0373 -->
> I keep having moments where I'm completely fine and then suddenly remember **“oh shit, I'm pregnant”** and start crying. Not because I want the pregnancy, I absolutely don't but because the situation itself is overwhelming and frightening.
<!-- /source-quote -->

**[pbu29-0054]**

<!-- source-quote:pbu29-0054 -->

**[pbu29-0211]**

<!-- source-quote:pbu29-0211 -->

I looked at the photo I took on Sunday I was bloated and tho it was the creatine till I found out it was pregnancy bloating today I'm not bloated it makes me sad. I'm sad it was a quick decision I wish I told myself to give time and keep the pregnancy.  
> I’ll cry if I find out there's nothing in my uterus and I'm no longer pregnant. I'm grieving this was my first pregnancy and first abortion.

### 9. A supportive partner can recognize that the bodily decision is not his.

This is a direct counterexample to reproductive pressure and birth-room entitlement; it does not erase his fear.

<!-- /source-quote -->

**[pbu29-0161]**

<!-- /source-quote -->

### 10. More measurements are not always the help the author needs.

One author reports improvement after putting checking devices away. Another cautions that a happy ending cannot forecast someone else's result, and explicitly rejects blaming a loss on lifestyle.

**[pbu06-0300]**

<!-- source-quote:pbu06-0300 -->
> I've realized that after the loss I've been treating every milestone like something to survive instead of something to celebrate. I've checked temperatures, heart rate, fertility hormones—you name it. Thankfully my husband finally hid all of those things because they were making my anxiety worse instead of helping.
> 
> This week I've been trying to replace the constant checking with long walks, breathing exercises, and positive affirmations. It's actually been helping.
<!-- /source-quote -->

**[pbu06-0383]**

<!-- source-quote:pbu06-0383 -->
> I know that one person’s happy ending cannot predict anyone else’s outcome. I also know that no amount of positivity can take away the fear of pregnancy after loss. But sometimes, I think it helps to hear from someone who has been through the heartbreak and eventually made it to the other side.
<!-- /source-quote -->

**[pbu06-0384]**

<!-- source-quote:pbu06-0384 -->
> And now, five days postpartum, I’m sitting here staring into my daughter’s beautiful eyes, wondering how I got so lucky. I know that changing my lifestyle does not guarantee a successful pregnancy. Plenty of women experience miscarriages while living incredibly healthy lives, and pregnancy loss is not a personal failure. There are so many possible reasons for miscarriage, and sometimes we never get the answers we desperately want. But I do think that losing weight, giving up alcohol, and prioritizing our health gave my partner and me a better foundation going into this pregnancy. I cannot say that those changes are what made the difference, and I would never want anyone to read my story and feel responsible for a loss they experienced.
<!-- /source-quote -->

### 11. The audience's requests for honesty point in opposite directions.

Some ask for all outcomes; others ask that miscarriage stories not be placed in front of them. This is evidence of different reader preferences, not a reason to remove evidence from research.

**[pbu06-0006]**

<!-- source-quote:pbu06-0006 -->

### 12. Calling every birth natural can itself feel like an unwanted platitude.

Findings §10 says to treat every kind of birth as normal; this account complicates the language used to do that. Respecting routes does not require overruling a parent's own description of violence or surgery.

**[pbu29-0126]**

<!-- source-quote:pbu29-0126 -->
> I had a c-section. It was literally the worst experience of my life. I was sliced open and  felt every puncture in every layer, felt my ribs, being pushed so my baby could be removed from my uterus, and every stitch required to jigsaw me back together. For months, the experience of my c-section overshadowed the birth of my son and I didn’t feel like it was worthwhile, a now I just don’t ask myself if it was worth it anymore. If there was some horrible magic that took me back to that point in order to have my child, I honestly don’t know how I’d respond.
<!-- /source-quote -->

**[pbu29-0127]**

<!-- source-quote:pbu29-0127 -->
> Now, it’s been 2 years and I’m pregnant again. I’m praying that I won’t have to have another c-section because I’m terrified of a repeat. I’m willing to give my body another try, but if I have another surgical birth, I’m never doing this again. There was NOTHING natural about my experience. I hesitate to even call it birth, and it irks my nerves to see the bleeding hearts come to my aid with unsolicited platitudes: “every birth is natural,” etc.  but it seems like I’m alone in this.
<!-- /source-quote -->

**[pbu29-0128]**

<!-- source-quote:pbu29-0128 -->
> I was a birth doula and worked in a hospital for years before I even got pregnant. Seeing so many pleasant vaginal births definitely informed my expectations and added on to my disappointment going into my c-section, but the actual experience felt like violence. Perhaps what I experienced wasn’t the norm, but I don’t see how anyone could consider c-section natural and it seems like I’m truly alone in this.
<!-- /source-quote -->

### 13. Purchased monitoring, caregivers or equipment can be both useful and unsettling.

The same purchase can bring relief, new safety worries, doubts about a refund, or another test interval. Acquisition alone is not value or a successful support plan.

### 14. There are explicit accounts of successful treatment, appreciated vaccination and reassuring outcomes.

Preserve these alongside fear and adverse outcomes. Attributed benefit is not an efficacy estimate; the supplement routine in Pattern 21 is likewise a parent's own explanation.

<!-- source-quote:pbu29-0368 -->
> I had a very scary week. I am 14 weeks pregnant and on Sunday I suddenly had an incident of heavy bleeding. I rushed to my hospital and my doctor immediately gave me an ultrasound and found pregnancy to be safe and the baby very healthy and active. I was so relieved. Doctor couldn't find any reason for the bleed and was so confused. I am APLA positive so I was on heparin and ecospirin. She immediately stopped my blood thinners and started Traxenamic acid in IV for three days. But just after day one of IV...my bleeding stopped and the issue resolved. Doctor checked my vagina and said there was no issue with the cervix or vagina too. So urine culture was taken. UTI was the culprit. Thankfully it was not too severe and my kidneys were alright. The doctor said UTI could've caused inflammation in my vagina and my blood thinners would've worsened it. I was immediately given three day antibiotics via IV and my condition improved. Now, augmentin has been prescribed along with cranberry juice for five days. It's been such a rollercoaster week for me. From crying hard to being so happy seeing my baby so healthy and active...uffff!!!
<!-- /source-quote -->

<!-- source-quote:pbu30-0260 -->

<!-- /source-quote -->

## What fits nowhere

The current headings can loosely hold parts of this material, but they do not give these decisions and situations a substantive home. The passages below identify what a rewrite would otherwise lose.

### 1. Deciding whether there will be a birth, including abortion, adoption and surrogacy.

Sections 1–10 mostly organize a pregnancy that continues. They have no sustained home for decisions made under legal deadlines, abortion certainty or regret, adoption agencies that do not answer, or fear that either pregnancy or surrogacy could be regretted. A new section must precede or sit alongside preparation, rather than treating every account as resistance to overcome.

### 2. Loss care and recognition after a baby will not come home.

The current historical gap is an acknowledgment of absence, not a substantive account of follow-up, physical depletion, memorials, having or not having held a stillborn child, and deciding to pause trying. Give these accounts their own home.

[pbu06-0073](#pbu06-0073) — full verbatim quotation in Pattern 3.

### 3. Safety, coercion and fear of disclosure to institutions.

A visitor plan or short partner format does not contain threats to kill family, stomach punching, nonconsensual ejaculation, feared CPS involvement, malicious reports, or lack of identity documents. These are distinct circumstances, not one diagnosis or one type of parent.

### 4. Dependence and help with stopping substances.

The historical selection table names substance use, but the main findings do not explain quitting while panicked, bereaved or without accessible care, buying nicotine patches, successful quitting, or losing covered MAT. Keep the actual language and competing fears.

### 5. Accessible newborn care before birth and housing that can be changed before injury.

Disability, stairs, transport trauma and bereavement change whether standard gear and discharge assumptions make sense. The accommodation problem can arise before a clinician is willing to document a restriction.

### 6. Pets, household safety and emotions toward animals.

The current sections can absorb distress, but do not have a clear home for preparing a home with an animal that bites, arguments about rehoming, rage toward dogs, or feeling rejected by a pet while depressed. These words should not disappear because they are socially uncomfortable.

### 8. Pregnancy during an external frightening event, and the everyday practical detail of bodily symptoms.

Running and jumping a fence while protecting a child becomes fear of harming the pregnancy. Constipation, an obstructed toilet, teeth/gums and intimate discomfort also have no useful home if everything is compressed into generic nausea or anxiety. Keep both the frightening and the mundane.

This entire quote field records only a check-up. Its id is accounted for, but it should not be treated as evidence of anxiety, loss, treatment quality or a request. Preserve it as a low-information extraction rather than attaching a conclusion the words do not establish.

## Money

Inclusive register: **178 distinct quote records** with a stated amount, cost/value judgment, actual or prospective purchase, hired/booked help, refund/exchange issue, gifted or promised goods, or a related financial constraint. This is not a count of transactions or parents. Acquisition is distinguished from payment by the source wording; an item used, given, sought or promised is not automatically a completed purchase. All amounts retain their original units and wording.

The few numeric statements need particular care: the $0 coverage and $10,000+ childbirth comparison are the author’s understanding [pbu06-0333]; $20 and $1500 are spending attributed to a partner [pbu29-0187]; $18.50/h is the bereaved parent’s reported wage [pbu29-0263]; $130 is groceries already bought [pbu29-0270]; “50k” and “20-30” are the income wording actually given [pbu29-0341]; $3600 is monthly household income [pbu29-0384]; “$70,000k” is literal quoted comparison language, not a corrected income statistic [pbu29-0386]; $350/month is reported financial support [pbu29-0415]; and “over 10k” is already committed to a wedding [pbu29-0424]. None prices a Mamivio guide.

> I always seen myself having a baby with someone loved and wanted a life with and now I'm pregnant and in this situation it's like I don't want it to grow up in a broken home life and neither of us are financially prepared I have absolutely no room for a baby and a roommate, he needs to get his own place as well, he said he would work two jobs in order to support us and everything before it comes but that's just not enough for me, I want to be somewhat ready and wanting a baby not a surprise like this. But being pregnant has made me realize how much I want a baby but it's like do I want a baby with this man? I'm also scared with how much guilt I will feel if I abort this baby, will i regret it the rest of my life? Will I know I made the right choice? Is it the right choice? Like I don't know I keep going back and forth and I don't have much time to take the pills before it's gonna be to late, I'm so scared to have this baby and not know if l'll be able to provide it with the life I want, I'm scared l'll regret not having it as I have no family or anything left it's just me and my god father.. im not getting any younger and he's a great guy I know he be a good father but I just so unsure of going through with this and it being a disaster, I watched my parents rage war with me in between and I can't have that for my child. I want a family and healthy family at that. I just need some advice, maybe stories? Anything to help me navigate this decision I have to make for myself. Thank you.
<!-- /source-quote -->

No new quote in this share states a purchase of the proposed Mamivio guide or a willingness to pay a particular amount for it. The willingness to pay out of pocket for an early scan [pbu29-0061], the worthwhile doula [pbu30-0037], the unaffordable repeated scans [pbu06-0188], the refund concern [pbu29-0088], and the unfulfilled half-payment promise [pbu30-0313] concern those specific services or costs.

## Requests

Inclusive register: **199 distinct quote records** covering direct asks, explicit wishes and reported attempts to obtain a product, plan, list, service or practical help. Some linked records supply the context or response to an ask rather than a separate request. These are not 199 independent requests or buyers. Repeated entries across topics are shown once; each id links to the full original words where already reproduced.

The requests range from a prenatal exercise plan, equipment and symptom relief to appointment wording, an OB discussion list, a birth plan, postpartum help, benefit applications, loss aftercare, abortion access and an adoptive family. Their desired outcome can differ from the proposed guide. Requests for personal experiences, positive stories, both good and bad outcomes, or reassurance are not in themselves requests to buy a product.

## Coverage and verification

Reading coverage was by contiguous record ranges, with the truncated UR29 passage reread separately: UR06 records 1–100, 101–200, 201–300, 301–396; UR29 records 1–60, 61–120, 121–180, 181–240, 241–300, 301–360, 361–432, plus an untruncated reread of ids ending 0393–0402; UR30 record positions 1–65, 66–130, 131–195, 196–260, 261–310, 311–339. UR30 record positions are not id suffixes because some allocated ids were burned. No share file or part of one was sampled.

Validation: every marked source block was decoded from Markdown blockquote prefixes and compared for full-string equality with that id’s quote field. Every source id anywhere in this file was checked against the union of the three assigned JSONL files. Current-findings quotations were checked as exact substrings of synthesis/findings.md. Repeated quotation instances were checked individually. No source quote was reconstructed from a ledger summary.

Coverage: 1,167 quotes in the share; 914 quotes reproduced verbatim (unique ids; 982 checked quotation instances); 253 further ids listed without a full reproduction (675 unique ids in further lists including overlaps with reproduced quotes); 0 ids not mentioned at all.
